  "README.md": {
    "path": "README.md",
    "contents": "<!--\n  generated from demo v1\n  model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n  contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n  do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n-->\n# demo in a browser\n\n**Do not edit these files.** They are synthesised from `examples/demo/` by `cargo xtask synth`, and CI fails if they differ from what the specification determines, if the module stops building for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, or if `index.html` calls an export the module does not have. Regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`.\n\nWhat is here:\n\n| file | what it is |\n| --- | --- |\n| `index.html` | the page. Every command, field, event, view and state on it is built from `catalog.json`; none of it is typed into the HTML |\n| `bridge.js` | the glue: reserve a buffer, write the request, call the module, read the response |\n| `catalog.json` | the model, as the page reads it — and the same bytes the module carries |\n| `crates/demo-web/` | the bridge crate: JSON in, JSON out, and the exports a browser calls |\n| `PLAN.md`, `plan.json` | the plan, byte-identical to every other target's |\n| `TARGET.md`, `target.json` | what a browser could not carry across the plan |\n\n## Building it\n\n```console\n$ rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown\n$ cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown\n```\n\nThat produces `target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/demo_web.wasm`. Every command will answer with the typed refusal naming an unmet obligation, because this tree implements none of them — that is the honest empty state, and the page shows the plan's own contract beside each one.\n\n## Running it against a realization\n\nA host crate that depends on this one as an `rlib`, links an implementation of every obligation, and exports `ess_realize` hands the assembled system to `install`. The exports below travel into that host's `cdylib`, so the same page drives it, and the page calls `ess_realize` if it is there. `examples/demo-web/` in this repository is that host.\n\nThe page looks for its module in three places, in order: the release build beside this file, the debug build, and `demo_web.wasm` in this directory. That last one is how a *realized* module is opened — copy it in under that name:\n\n```console\n$ (cd ../../../examples/demo-web && cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown)\n$ cp ../../../examples/demo-web/target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/*.wasm ./demo_web.wasm\n```\n\nThen serve this directory and open `index.html` — a browser will not instantiate WebAssembly from a `file://` URL:\n\n```console\n$ python3 -m http.server\n$ open http://localhost:8000/index.html\n```\n\n## The exports\n\n| export | what it does |\n| --- | --- |\n| `ess_input_reserve` | reserves a buffer of the given length for the next request and answers its address in linear memory |\n| `ess_dispatch` | serves the request in that buffer and answers the address of the JSON response |\n| `ess_output_len` | the length in bytes of the response just produced |\n| `ess_realize` | optional, and never in this module: a host that links a realization exports it, and the page calls it if it is there |\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },
  "TARGET.md": {
    "path": "TARGET.md",
    "contents": "<!--\n  generated from demo v1\n  model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n  contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n  do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n-->\n# Target notes — web\n\nFor demo v1. The `PLAN.md` beside this file is language-neutral and **byte-identical in every target's tree**; this document is what *this* target could not carry across it. Regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`.\n\n6 weakening(s), 0 target refusal(s). A weakening is emitted code that holds less than the first target's; a target refusal is a capability the plan marks generated and this language cannot represent — a fact about the language, never about the specification.\n\n## Weakened — emitted, with less than the first target holds\n\n| the guarantee | what this target provides | capabilities affected |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| the generated crate forbids `unsafe`, so the compiler closes the question rather than a reader checking it | a WebAssembly export is a `#[no_mangle]` item, and rustc's own `unsafe_code` lint flags one — so the bridge crate cannot declare `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` and declares `#![deny(missing_docs)]` alone. It contains no `unsafe` block, no `unsafe fn` and no raw-pointer dereference; what is lost is the compiler closing the question, not the property | component port, command contract |\n| a move the lifecycle does not declare does not compile | the page speaks JSON, and JSON carries no type parameter: any declared command can be sent from any state, and an illegal move comes back as the declared refusal the behaviour answers with — at run time, from the system, rather than as a build that failed. The typed lifecycle still holds inside the system this bridge drives; it simply does not reach across the boundary | entity lifecycle, command contract |\n| the current state of every instance is observable | the synthesised system holds no entity store — where instances live is an obligation — so the page shows each declared view's rows beside the entity's declared lifecycle, and shows a per-instance state only where a view projects one. Deriving a state from the event log would be behaviour, and behaviour is not synthesised | entity lifecycle, view type |\n| an `Integer` is sixty-four bits wide, end to end | the bridge writes it as a JSON number, which is what the published wire contract fixes, and a browser reads every JSON number as a double — so a magnitude past 2^53 is rounded by the page. The bridge itself never truncates: a fraction, an exponent or an out-of-range magnitude arriving from the page is refused with the path it was found at | domain type, command contract, event type, error type, view type |\n| a generated tree builds from exactly its committed bytes, outside this repository | this tree is a front end over the Rust target's crates, so its manifest names them by relative path — `../../rust/<system>/` from this tree's root, which is the layout `cargo xtask synth` commits. Copy both trees or neither; a browser realization on its own has no system to drive | domain type, entity lifecycle, command contract, event type, error type, view type, conversion, binding transformation, binding delivery, component port |\n| a binding whose failure policy is `retry` is redelivered on the schedule the transport provides | the page is the transport's caller, and nothing here advances a clock: redelivery is a request a person makes, one occurrence at a time, and the duplicate `at_least_once` permits is something to watch rather than something to wait for. When to try again is a deployment decision the specification does not take, and this target does not take it either | binding delivery |\n\n## Refused by this target — planned, not emitted\n\n| capability | source | why |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },
  "bridge.js": {
    "path": "bridge.js",
    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n// contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n\n// The boundary, written once. The page imports it, and so does the smoke test that loads the\n// module outside a browser — so what the test exercises is the page's own glue and not a second\n// implementation that happens to agree with it.\n//\n// Three exports and nothing else. `memory.buffer` is read afresh on every access because\n// allocating inside the module may grow the memory, which detaches every view taken before it.\n\n/**\n * Instantiates a module and answers a driver over it.\n *\n * `source` is anything `WebAssembly.instantiate` accepts: the bytes of a `.wasm`, or a compiled\n * `Module`. If the module exports the optional realization hook, it is called once, before any\n * request — that is how a host that linked implementations of the plan's obligations reaches a\n * page neither of them was written against.\n */\nexport async function open(source) {\n  const { instance } = await WebAssembly.instantiate(source, {});\n  const exports = instance.exports;\n  for (const name of EXPORTS) {\n    if (typeof exports[name] !== \"function\") {\n      throw new Error(`the module does not export ${name}; the page and the module disagree`);\n    }\n  }\n  const realized = typeof exports[REALIZE] === \"function\";\n  if (realized) exports[REALIZE]();\n\n  const encoder = new TextEncoder();\n  const decoder = new TextDecoder();\n\n  function request(body) {\n    const bytes = encoder.encode(JSON.stringify(body));\n    const address = exports.ess_input_reserve(bytes.length);\n    new Uint8Array(exports.memory.buffer, address, bytes.length).set(bytes);\n    const at = exports.ess_dispatch();\n    const length = exports.ess_output_len();\n    const text = decoder.decode(new Uint8Array(exports.memory.buffer, at, length));\n    return JSON.parse(text);\n  }\n\n  return { request, realized, exports };\n}\n\nexport const EXPORTS = [\"ess_input_reserve\", \"ess_dispatch\", \"ess_output_len\"];\n\nexport const REALIZE = \"ess_realize\";\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },
  "catalog.json": {
    "path": "catalog.json",
    "contents": "{\n  \"bindings\": [],\n  \"commands\": [\n    {\n      \"behavior\": {\n        \"contract\": \"given `demo.core.Fire` input, decide and enact exactly one outcome — `done` otherwise, emits `demo.core.Fired`\",\n        \"disposition\": \"obligation\",\n        \"why\": \"the contract is declared; the algorithm is not\"\n      },\n      \"component\": \"json\",\n      \"dispatchable\": true,\n      \"display\": \"Fire\",\n      \"domain\": \"demo.core\",\n      \"input\": [],\n      \"name\": \"demo.core.Fire\",\n      \"outcomes\": [\n        {\n          \"condition\": \"otherwise\",\n          \"error\": null,\n          \"moves\": null,\n          \"name\": \"done\",\n          \"publishes\": [\n            \"demo.core.Fired\"\n          ],\n          \"summary\": null\n        }\n      ]\n    }\n  ],\n  \"components\": [\n    {\n      \"accepts\": [\n        \"demo.core.Fire\"\n      ],\n      \"display\": \"json\",\n      \"name\": \"json\",\n      \"publishes\": [\n        \"demo.core.Fired\"\n      ],\n      \"summary\": null\n    }\n  ],\n  \"conversions\": [],\n  \"display\": \"demo\",\n  \"entities\": [],\n  \"errors\": [],\n  \"events\": [\n    {\n      \"display\": \"Fired\",\n      \"fields\": [],\n      \"name\": \"demo.core.Fired\",\n      \"summary\": null\n    }\n  ],\n  \"format\": \"ess-browser-catalog/1\",\n  \"plan\": {\n    \"generated\": 3,\n    \"obligations\": 1,\n    \"refused\": 0\n  },\n  \"provenance\": {\n    \"contract_digest\": \"6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\",\n    \"source_digest\": \"62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\",\n    \"specification_version\": \"v1\",\n    \"system\": \"demo\"\n  },\n  \"system\": \"demo\",\n  \"types\": {},\n  \"version\": \"v1\",\n  \"views\": []\n}\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },
  "crates/demo-web/Cargo.toml": {
    "path": "crates/demo-web/Cargo.toml",
    "contents": "# generated from demo v1\n# model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n# contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n# do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n\n[package]\nname = \"demo-web\"\ndescription = \"The `demo` system, v1, behind a WebAssembly boundary: JSON in, JSON out, and the catalogue a page renders itself from.\"\nversion = \"1.0.0\"\nedition = \"2021\"\n\n# Both, and both are used. `cdylib` is what a browser instantiates. `rlib` is what a host crate links against to install its own realization — the exports below travel into that host's own `cdylib`, so a realized module and this one answer the same page.\n[lib]\ncrate-type = [\"cdylib\", \"rlib\"]\n\n# Path dependencies into the Rust target's committed tree, and no third party at all: `cargo build` inside this tree is a gate step, and a step that resolves a crate is a step that reaches the network.\n[dependencies]\ndemo-system = { path = \"../../../../rust/demo/crates/demo-system\" }\ndemo-types = { path = \"../../../../rust/demo/crates/demo-types\" }\njson = { path = \"../../../../rust/demo/crates/json\" }\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },
  "crates/demo-web/src/catalog.rs": {
    "path": "crates/demo-web/src/catalog.rs",
    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n// contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n\n//! The model this page renders itself from.\n//!\n//! Pulled in from `catalog.json` beside the tree root rather than written here, so a reviewer reads the\n//! catalogue as JSON and the module carries it without a second copy. The page asks the running\n//! system for it — a page opened from `file://` can read its own WebAssembly module and cannot\n//! always read its neighbours.\n\n/// The model, as canonical JSON.\npub const CATALOG: &str = include_str!(\"../../../catalog.json\");\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },
  "crates/demo-web/src/json.rs": {
    "path": "crates/demo-web/src/json.rs",
    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n// contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n\n//! JSON at this system's boundary: a reader, a writer, and the base64 codec `Bytes` needs.\n//!\n//! Written here rather than taken from a crate because this workspace has no dependencies: it is\n//! built inside a gate that reaches no network. The surface is exactly what the generated `wire`\n//! module beside it uses — nothing general, nothing speculative.\n\nuse std::fmt;\n\n/// How deep a document from a caller may nest before it is refused.\n///\n/// A caller can send anything, and a recursive reader with no limit turns a hostile document\n/// into a stack overflow — which is a crash the process cannot catch and cannot report. Sixty-four is far past any model this emitter can produce: the specification's own\n/// type references are refused past thirty-two.\nconst DEPTH: usize = 64;\n\n/// A JSON value, as it arrived.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]\npub enum Value {\n    /// `null`.\n    Null,\n    /// `true` or `false`.\n    Bool(bool),\n    /// A number, kept in the spelling it arrived in rather than parsed into a float.\n    ///\n    /// A float would round a decimal the model deliberately carries as text, and this reader has\n    /// no business deciding that `10.50` is `10.5`.\n    Number(String),\n    /// A string, with its escapes resolved.\n    Text(String),\n    /// An array, in order.\n    Array(Vec<Value>),\n    /// An object, in the order its members arrived.\n    ///\n    /// A list of pairs rather than a map: order is what makes a rendering reproducible, and a\n    /// duplicate key is a fact about the document rather than something to silently collapse.\n    Object(Vec<(String, Value)>),\n}\n\nimpl Value {\n    /// The member with this name, or `None` — including when this is not an object at all.\n    pub fn member(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&Self> {\n        match self {\n            Self::Object(members) => members\n                .iter()\n                .find(|(key, _)| key == name)\n                .map(|(_, value)| value),\n            _ => None,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// What this value is, in one word, for a message about what was expected instead.\n    pub fn describes(&self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            Self::Null => \"null\",\n            Self::Bool(_) => \"a boolean\",\n            Self::Number(_) => \"a number\",\n            Self::Text(_) => \"a string\",\n            Self::Array(_) => \"an array\",\n            Self::Object(_) => \"an object\",\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// A document the reader refused, and where.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct ParseError {\n    /// The byte offset it stopped at.\n    pub at: usize,\n    /// What would have been legal there.\n    pub expected: String,\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for ParseError {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        write!(f, \"at byte {}: expected {}\", self.at, self.expected)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads one JSON document.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`ParseError`] naming the offset and what was expected there, for anything that is not exactly\n/// one well-formed value followed by whitespace.\npub fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    let bytes = text.as_bytes();\n    let mut at = 0;\n    skip(bytes, &mut at);\n    let value = read(bytes, &mut at, 0)?;\n    skip(bytes, &mut at);\n    if at != bytes.len() {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at,\n            expected: \"the end of the document\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    }\n    Ok(value)\n}\n\n/// Advances past whitespace.\nfn skip(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) {\n    while let Some(byte) = bytes.get(*at) {\n        if matches!(byte, b' ' | b'\\t' | b'\\n' | b'\\r') {\n            *at += 1;\n        } else {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads one value at `at`, which is already past any leading whitespace.\nfn read(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize, depth: usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    if depth > DEPTH {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"a document nested less than 64 deep\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    }\n    match bytes.get(*at) {\n        Some(b'{') => object(bytes, at, depth),\n        Some(b'[') => array(bytes, at, depth),\n        Some(b'\"') => text(bytes, at).map(Value::Text),\n        Some(b't') => literal(bytes, at, b\"true\", Value::Bool(true)),\n        Some(b'f') => literal(bytes, at, b\"false\", Value::Bool(false)),\n        Some(b'n') => literal(bytes, at, b\"null\", Value::Null),\n        Some(byte) if *byte == b'-' || byte.is_ascii_digit() => number(bytes, at),\n        _ => Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"a value\".to_owned(),\n        }),\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads one of the three bare words.\nfn literal(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize, word: &[u8], value: Value) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    if bytes.len() < *at + word.len() || &bytes[*at..*at + word.len()] != word {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: format!(\"`{}`\", String::from_utf8_lossy(word)),\n        });\n    }\n    *at += word.len();\n    Ok(value)\n}\n\n/// Reads a number, keeping its spelling.\nfn number(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    let start = *at;\n    if bytes.get(*at) == Some(&b'-') {\n        *at += 1;\n    }\n    let digits = *at;\n    while bytes.get(*at).is_some_and(u8::is_ascii_digit) {\n        *at += 1;\n    }\n    if *at == digits {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"a digit\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    }\n    if bytes.get(*at) == Some(&b'.') {\n        *at += 1;\n        let fraction = *at;\n        while bytes.get(*at).is_some_and(u8::is_ascii_digit) {\n            *at += 1;\n        }\n        if *at == fraction {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"a digit after the decimal point\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n    }\n    if matches!(bytes.get(*at), Some(b'e' | b'E')) {\n        *at += 1;\n        if matches!(bytes.get(*at), Some(b'+' | b'-')) {\n            *at += 1;\n        }\n        let exponent = *at;\n        while bytes.get(*at).is_some_and(u8::is_ascii_digit) {\n            *at += 1;\n        }\n        if *at == exponent {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"a digit in the exponent\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(Value::Number(\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&bytes[start..*at]).into_owned(),\n    ))\n}\n\n/// Reads a string, resolving its escapes.\nfn text(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<String, ParseError> {\n    *at += 1;\n    let mut out = String::new();\n    loop {\n        let Some(byte) = bytes.get(*at).copied() else {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"a closing quote\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        };\n        match byte {\n            b'\"' => {\n                *at += 1;\n                return Ok(out);\n            }\n            b'\\\\' => {\n                *at += 1;\n                let escaped = escape(bytes, at)?;\n                out.push(escaped);\n            }\n            _ => {\n                let start = *at;\n                while bytes\n                    .get(*at)\n                    .is_some_and(|byte| *byte != b'\"' && *byte != b'\\\\')\n                {\n                    *at += 1;\n                }\n                out.push_str(&String::from_utf8_lossy(&bytes[start..*at]));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads one escape sequence, the backslash already consumed.\nfn escape(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<char, ParseError> {\n    let Some(byte) = bytes.get(*at).copied() else {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"an escape\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    };\n    *at += 1;\n    Ok(match byte {\n        b'\"' => '\"',\n        b'\\\\' => '\\\\',\n        b'/' => '/',\n        b'b' => '\\u{8}',\n        b'f' => '\\u{c}',\n        b'n' => '\\n',\n        b'r' => '\\r',\n        b't' => '\\t',\n        b'u' => return unicode(bytes, at),\n        _ => {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"one of \\\" \\\\ / b f n r t u\".to_owned(),\n            })\n        }\n    })\n}\n\n/// Reads a `\\u` escape, joining a surrogate pair when it finds one.\nfn unicode(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<char, ParseError> {\n    let first = hex(bytes, at)?;\n    if (0xD800..0xDC00).contains(&first) {\n        // A lone high surrogate is not a character; the low half has to follow, escaped the same\n        // way. Refusing here rather than substituting a replacement character keeps a truncated\n        // document a refusal instead of a value nobody sent.\n        if bytes.get(*at) != Some(&b'\\\\') || bytes.get(*at + 1) != Some(&b'u') {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"the low half of a surrogate pair\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n        *at += 2;\n        let second = hex(bytes, at)?;\n        if !(0xDC00..0xE000).contains(&second) {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"the low half of a surrogate pair\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n        let joined = 0x1_0000 + ((first - 0xD800) << 10) + (second - 0xDC00);\n        return char::from_u32(joined).ok_or(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"a character\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    }\n    char::from_u32(first).ok_or(ParseError {\n        at: *at,\n        expected: \"a character\".to_owned(),\n    })\n}\n\n/// Reads exactly four hexadecimal digits.\nfn hex(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<u32, ParseError> {\n    let mut value = 0;\n    for _ in 0..4 {\n        let Some(byte) = bytes.get(*at).copied() else {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"four hexadecimal digits\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        };\n        let digit = match byte {\n            b'0'..=b'9' => u32::from(byte - b'0'),\n            b'a'..=b'f' => u32::from(byte - b'a') + 10,\n            b'A'..=b'F' => u32::from(byte - b'A') + 10,\n            _ => {\n                return Err(ParseError {\n                    at: *at,\n                    expected: \"four hexadecimal digits\".to_owned(),\n                })\n            }\n        };\n        value = value * 16 + digit;\n        *at += 1;\n    }\n    Ok(value)\n}\n\n/// Reads an array.\nfn array(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize, depth: usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    *at += 1;\n    let mut items = Vec::new();\n    skip(bytes, at);\n    if bytes.get(*at) == Some(&b']') {\n        *at += 1;\n        return Ok(Value::Array(items));\n    }\n    loop {\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        items.push(read(bytes, at, depth + 1)?);\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        match bytes.get(*at) {\n            Some(b',') => *at += 1,\n            Some(b']') => {\n                *at += 1;\n                return Ok(Value::Array(items));\n            }\n            _ => {\n                return Err(ParseError {\n                    at: *at,\n                    expected: \"`,` or `]`\".to_owned(),\n                })\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads an object.\nfn object(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize, depth: usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    *at += 1;\n    let mut members = Vec::new();\n    skip(bytes, at);\n    if bytes.get(*at) == Some(&b'}') {\n        *at += 1;\n        return Ok(Value::Object(members));\n    }\n    loop {\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        if bytes.get(*at) != Some(&b'\"') {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"a member name\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n        let name = text(bytes, at)?;\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        if bytes.get(*at) != Some(&b':') {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"`:`\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n        *at += 1;\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        members.push((name, read(bytes, at, depth + 1)?));\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        match bytes.get(*at) {\n            Some(b',') => *at += 1,\n            Some(b'}') => {\n                *at += 1;\n                return Ok(Value::Object(members));\n            }\n            _ => {\n                return Err(ParseError {\n                    at: *at,\n                    expected: \"`,` or `}`\".to_owned(),\n                })\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ---- writing -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Appends a JSON string, escaped.\npub fn push_text(out: &mut String, text: &str) {\n    out.push('\"');\n    for character in text.chars() {\n        match character {\n            '\"' => out.push_str(\"\\\\\\\"\"),\n            '\\\\' => out.push_str(\"\\\\\\\\\"),\n            '\\n' => out.push_str(\"\\\\n\"),\n            '\\r' => out.push_str(\"\\\\r\"),\n            '\\t' => out.push_str(\"\\\\t\"),\n            // Every other control character has to be escaped for the document to be legal, and\n            // `\\u` is the only form JSON has for one.\n            character if (character as u32) < 0x20 => {\n                out.push_str(&format!(\"\\\\u{:04x}\", character as u32));\n            }\n            character => out.push(character),\n        }\n    }\n    out.push('\"');\n}\n\n/// Appends a JSON number.\npub fn push_integer(out: &mut String, value: i64) {\n    out.push_str(&value.to_string());\n}\n\n/// Appends `true` or `false`.\npub fn push_bool(out: &mut String, value: bool) {\n    out.push_str(if value { \"true\" } else { \"false\" });\n}\n\n/// Appends opaque bytes as the base64 string the published wire contracts fix.\npub fn push_base64(out: &mut String, bytes: &[u8]) {\n    let mut encoded = String::new();\n    for chunk in bytes.chunks(3) {\n        let a = u32::from(chunk[0]);\n        let b = chunk.get(1).copied().map_or(0, u32::from);\n        let c = chunk.get(2).copied().map_or(0, u32::from);\n        let packed = (a << 16) | (b << 8) | c;\n        encoded.push(ALPHABET[(packed >> 18) as usize & 63] as char);\n        encoded.push(ALPHABET[(packed >> 12) as usize & 63] as char);\n        encoded.push(if chunk.len() > 1 {\n            ALPHABET[(packed >> 6) as usize & 63] as char\n        } else {\n            '='\n        });\n        encoded.push(if chunk.len() > 2 {\n            ALPHABET[packed as usize & 63] as char\n        } else {\n            '='\n        });\n    }\n    push_text(out, &encoded);\n}\n\n/// The standard base64 alphabet, padded — the encoding the JSON Schema projection's pattern checks.\nconst ALPHABET: &[u8; 64] = b\"ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789+/\";\n\n// ---- reading into the model's own shapes ---------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// A value that was not what the model's declaration says it is.\n///\n/// Carries the path it was reached at, because a caller sending a nested command input gets one\n/// message and \"expected a string\" without a path is a message nobody can act on.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct DecodeError {\n    /// Where in the document, as a dotted path from its root.\n    pub at: String,\n    /// What the declaration says belongs there.\n    pub expected: String,\n    /// What was there instead.\n    pub found: String,\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for DecodeError {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        write!(f, \"{}: expected {}, found {}\", self.at, self.expected, self.found)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl DecodeError {\n    /// A refusal at one path, with what was expected and what arrived.\n    pub fn of(at: &str, expected: &str, found: &Value) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            at: at.to_owned(),\n            expected: expected.to_owned(),\n            found: found.describes().to_owned(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// One step further into a document, for a message a reader can follow back.\npub fn nested(at: &str, step: &str) -> String {\n    if at.is_empty() {\n        step.to_owned()\n    } else {\n        format!(\"{at}.{step}\")\n    }\n}\n\n/// The string at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is anything else.\npub fn text_at<'a>(value: &'a Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<&'a str, DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Text(text) => Ok(text),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The integer at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is not a number, or is one no `Integer` can hold — a fraction,\n/// an exponent, or a magnitude past 64 bits. Refused rather than rounded: a silently truncated\n/// identifier is worse than a rejected command.\npub fn integer_at(value: &Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<i64, DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Number(number) => number.parse::<i64>().map_err(|_| DecodeError {\n            at: at.to_owned(),\n            expected: expected.to_owned(),\n            found: format!(\"the number {number}\"),\n        }),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The boolean at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is anything else.\npub fn bool_at(value: &Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<bool, DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Bool(flag) => Ok(*flag),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The bytes at this path, read from base64.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is not a string, or not base64.\npub fn bytes_at(value: &Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, DecodeError> {\n    let text = text_at(value, at, expected)?;\n    base64(text, at, expected)\n}\n\n/// Base64 text as bytes.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when a character is outside the alphabet.\nfn base64(text: &str, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, DecodeError> {\n    let mut packed = 0_u32;\n    let mut held = 0_u32;\n    let mut out = Vec::new();\n    for byte in text.bytes() {\n        if byte == b'=' {\n            break;\n        }\n        let Some(index) = ALPHABET.iter().position(|candidate| *candidate == byte) else {\n            return Err(DecodeError {\n                at: at.to_owned(),\n                expected: expected.to_owned(),\n                found: \"a string that is not base64\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        };\n        packed = (packed << 6) | index as u32;\n        held += 6;\n        if held >= 8 {\n            held -= 8;\n            out.push(((packed >> held) & 0xFF) as u8);\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(out)\n}\n\n/// The integer a map key spells.\n///\n/// JSON has only string keys, and the published wire contract constrains a non-string map's\n/// `propertyNames` to text of the declared primitive's shape. This is that constraint, applied\n/// where the value is actually built.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the key is not an integer's spelling.\npub fn key_integer(key: &str, at: &str) -> Result<i64, DecodeError> {\n    key.parse::<i64>().map_err(|_| DecodeError {\n        at: at.to_owned(),\n        expected: \"a key spelling an integer\".to_owned(),\n        found: format!(\"the key `{key}`\"),\n    })\n}\n\n/// The boolean a map key spells.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the key is neither `true` nor `false`.\npub fn key_bool(key: &str, at: &str) -> Result<bool, DecodeError> {\n    match key {\n        \"true\" => Ok(true),\n        \"false\" => Ok(false),\n        other => Err(DecodeError {\n            at: at.to_owned(),\n            expected: \"a key spelling `true` or `false`\".to_owned(),\n            found: format!(\"the key `{other}`\"),\n        }),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The bytes a base64 map key spells.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the key is not base64.\npub fn key_bytes(key: &str, at: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, DecodeError> {\n    base64(key, at, \"a base64 key\")\n}\n\n/// Opens a member of the object currently being written, with the separator it needs.\n///\n/// The separator is decided from the buffer rather than from a flag every generated writer would\n/// have to thread through every branch: an absent optional field is *omitted*, exactly as the\n/// published contract's `required` list says, and a comma written before nothing is a document no\n/// reader accepts. A complete JSON value never ends with `{`, so the buffer ending with one means\n/// this is the first member and nothing else.\npub fn member(out: &mut String, name: &str) {\n    if !out.ends_with('{') {\n        out.push(',');\n    }\n    push_text(out, name);\n    out.push(':');\n}\n\n/// The array at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is anything else.\npub fn items_at<'a>(value: &'a Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<&'a [Value], DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Array(items) => Ok(items),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The object members at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is anything else.\npub fn members_at<'a>(\n    value: &'a Value,\n    at: &str,\n    expected: &str,\n) -> Result<&'a [(String, Value)], DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Object(members) => Ok(members),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The named member of the object at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is not an object, or the member is absent — absence of a\n/// required field is a refusal, never a default.\npub fn member_at<'a>(value: &'a Value, at: &str, name: &str) -> Result<&'a Value, DecodeError> {\n    let members = members_at(value, at, \"an object\")?;\n    members\n        .iter()\n        .find(|(key, _)| key == name)\n        .map(|(_, member)| member)\n        .ok_or_else(|| DecodeError {\n            at: nested(at, name),\n            expected: \"a value\".to_owned(),\n            found: \"nothing\".to_owned(),\n        })\n}\n",

The `demo` system, v1, behind a WebAssembly boundary.\n//!\n//! Generated, not written: the specification is the source of truth, and the door to changing\n//! anything here is `ess synthesize --target web`. This crate emits no behaviour — every\n//! command behaviour, view projection and escalation is an obligation, listed with its contract\n//! in the `PLAN.md` beside this tree. With nothing installed the page runs against the generated\n//! stubs and every command answers with the typed refusal naming what is owed.\n//!\n//! # No `forbid(unsafe_code)`, and why\n//!\n//! A WebAssembly export is a `#[no_mangle]` item, which rustc's `unsafe_code` lint flags — so the\n//! lint every other generated crate here forbids cannot be declared in this one. \"this crate is a browser realization: build it with `--target wasm32-unknown-unknown`\"\n);\n\npub mod catalog;\npub mod json;\npub mod wire;\n\nuse std::cell::RefCell;\n\n/// Why a request could not be served.\n///\n/// Every variant is a *value*: a WebAssembly trap tells a page nothing beyond \"it failed\", so\n/// nothing below panics and nothing below unwraps a caller's input.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum BridgeError {\n    /// The request was not well-formed JSON.\n    Malformed(json::ParseError),\n    /// The request named no kind this bridge serves.\n    UnknownRequest(String),\n    /// The request named a command this system does not declare, or this target cannot dispatch.\n    UnknownCommand(String),\n    /// The input did not match the type the /// The capability kind, as the plan spells it.\n        capability: String,\n        /// The construct that requires it, in the specification's own spelling.\n        source: String,\n    },\n    /// than\n    /// display it.\n    pub fn encode(&self, out: &mut String) {\n        out.push('{');\n        match self {\n            Self::Malformed(error) => {\n                json::member(out, \"kind\");\n                json::push_text(out, \"malformed\");\n                json::member(out, \"detail\");\n                json::push_text(out, &error.to_string());\n            }\n            Self::UnknownRequest(kind) => {\n                json::member(out, \"kind\");\n                json::push_text(out, \"unknown-request\");\n                json::member(out, \"request\");\n \"unknown-command\");\n                json::member(out, \"command\");\n                json::push_text(out, command);\n            }\n            Self::Undecodable(error) => {\n                json::member(out, \"kind\");\n                json::push_text(out, \"undecodable\");\n                json::member(out, \"at\");\n                json::push_text(out, &error.at);\n                json::member(out, \"expected\");\n                json::push_text(out, &error.expected);\n                json::member(out, \"found\");\n                json::push_text(out, &error.found);\n            }\n            Self::Unmet { capability, source } => {\n                json::member(out, \"kind\");\n                json::push_text(out, \"unmet-obligation\");\n                json::member(out, \"capability\");\n                json::push_text(out, capability);\n                json::member(out, \"source\");\n                json::push_text(out, source);\n            }\n            Self::NoSuchOccurrence(occurrence) => {\n                json::member(out, \"kind\");\n                json::push_text(out, \"no-such-occurrence\");\n                json::member(out, from(error: json::DecodeError) -> Self {\n        Self::Undecodable(error)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl From<demo_types::obligation::UnmetObligation> for BridgeError {\n    fn from(unmet: }\n}\n\n/// The running system, behind a boundary that erases which realization assembled it.\n///\n/// Implemented once below, generically, over the generated `System` — so a host links its own\n/// implementations of every obligation, hands the assembled system to [`install`], and this\n/// bridge never chooses one. Zero implementations for an obligation is an unsatisfied\n/// obligation and two is an ambiguity; neither is a decision for the machinery (gap register\n/// D-2), so there is no registry and no default beyond the generated stubs that refuse.\npub trait Bound {\n    /// Runs one declared command from its JSON input, then pumps the transport until quiescent.\n    ///\n    /// # Errors\n    ///\n    /// [`BridgeError`] for a command this system does not accept, an input that does not match\n    /// the declared type, or an obligation nothing has satisfied. A *declared* refusal is not an\n    /// error: it comes back as the outcome it is.\n    fn run(&mut self, command: &str, input: &json::Value) -> Result<String, BridgeError>;\n\n    /// Delivers one already-published occurrence again — the duplicate `at_least_once` permits.\n    ///\n    /// # Errors\n    ///\n    /// [`BridgeError::NoSuchOccurrence`] for an index the log does not hold, and\n    /// [`BridgeError::Unmet`] for an obligation redelivery reached.\n    fn replay(&mut self, occurrence: usize) -> Result<(), BridgeError>;\n\n    /// Everything published so far, in publication order — the system's observable record.\n    fn log(&self) -> String;\n\n    /// Every command a binding invoked, with the input it passed.\n    fn invoked(&self) -> String;\n\n    /// Every declared view's rows, or the refusal serving it answered with.\n    fn projected(&self) -> String;\n}\n\nimpl<JsonBehaviors> Bound for demo_system::System<JsonBehaviors>\nwhere\n    JsonBehaviors: demo_types::core::obligations::FireBehavior,\n{\n    fn run(&mut self, command: &str, input: &json::Value) -> Result<String, BridgeError> {\n        let mut out = String::new();\n        match command {\n            \"demo.core.Fire\" => {\n                let input = wire::decode_command_demo_core_fire(input, \"input\")?;\n                let outcome = self.json.fire(input)?;\n                wire::encode_outcome_demo_core_fire(&outcome, &mut out);\n            }\n            other => return Err(BridgeError::UnknownCommand(other.to_owned())),\n        }\n        self.pump()?;\n        Ok(out)\n    }\n\n    fn replay(&mut self, occurrence: usize) -> Result<(), BridgeError> {\n        if demo_system::System::published(self).get(occurrence).is_none() {\n            return Err(BridgeError::NoSuchOccurrence(occurrence));\n        }\n        self.pump()?;\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    fn log(&self) -> String {\n        let mut out = String::new();\n        out.push('[');\n        for (occurrence, event) in demo_system::System::published(self).iter().enumerate() {\n            if occurrence > 0 {\n                out.push(',');\n            }\n            out.push('{');\n            json::member(&mut out, \"occurrence\");\n            json::push_integer(&mut out, occurrence as i64);\n            json::member(&mut out, \"event\");\n            match event {\n                demo_system::SystemEvent::Fired(payload) => {\n                    json::push_text(&mut out, \"demo.core.Fired\");\n                    json::member(&mut out, \"payload\");\n construction.\n        \"[]\".to_owned()\n    }\n\n    fn projected(&self) -> String {\n        let mut out = String::new();\n        out.push('{');\n        out.push('}');\n        out\n    }\n}\n\n/// Every obligation of this system, refused in the type system.\n///\n/// Not a second copy of the generated stubs: each method below delegates to the one the\n/// Rust target emitted, so the refusal a page shows is the plan entry that target names. Installing twice replaces: the last system installed is the one\n/// serving, and there is no merge, because merging two realizations would be choosing between\n/// them.\npub fn install(system: Box<dyn Bound>) {\n    SYSTEM.with(|held| {\n        *held.borrow_mut() = Some(system);\n    });\n}\n\n/// The system nobody has realized: generated ports over stubs that refuse.\n///\n/// The honest empty state. Every command answers with the typed refusal naming what is owed,\n/// which is exactly what `PLAN.md` says is owed.\nfn unrealized() -> Box<dyn Bound> {\n    Box::new(demo_system::System::new(json::Json::new(Unrealized)))\n}\n\n/// Runs one action against the installed system, installing the unrealized one if none is.\nfn with_system<T>(action: impl FnOnce(&mut dyn Bound) -> T) -> T {\n    SYSTEM.with(|held| {\n        let mut held = held.borrow_mut();\n        let system = held.get_or_insert_with(unrealized);\n        action(system.as_mut())\n    })\n}\n\n/// Serves one request, answering JSON whatever happens.\n///\n/// Four requests, and the protocol is the same for every specification:\n///\n/// | request | what comes back |\n/// | --- | --- |\n/// | `{\"request\":\"catalog\"}` | the model this page renders itself from |\n/// | `{\"request\":\"observe\"}` | the log, the binding invocations, and every view's rows |\n/// | `{\"request\":\"command\",\"command\":…,\"input\":{…}}` | the outcome, then the same observation |\n/// | `{\"request\":\"redeliver\",\"occurrence\":n}` | the occurrence delivered again, then the observation |\n///\n/// A refusal comes back as `{\"ok\":false,\"error\":{…}}` with a `kind` a page can react to, never as\n/// a trap.\npub fn serve(request: &str) -> String {\n    let mut out = String::new();\n    match answer(request, &mut out) {\n        Ok(()) => out,\n        Err(error) => {\n            let mut refusal = String::new();\n            refusal.push('{');\n            json::member(&mut refusal, \"ok\");\n            json::push_bool(&mut refusal, false);\n            json::member(&mut refusal, \"error\");\n            error.encode(&mut refusal);\n            refusal.push('}');\n            refusal\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// One request, served into `out`.\nfn answer(request: &str, out: &mut String) -> Result<(), BridgeError> {\n    let request = json::parse(request).map_err(BridgeError::Malformed)?;\n    let kind = json::text_at(\n        json::member_at(&request, \"\", \"request\")?,\n        \"request\",\n        \"a request kind\",\n    )?\n    .to_owned();\n    out.push('{');\n    json::member(out, \"ok\");\n    json::push_bool(out, true);\n    match kind.as_str() {\n        \"catalog\" => {\n            json::member(out, \"catalog\");\n            out.push_str(catalog::CATALOG);\n        }\n        \"observe\" => observe(out),\n        \"command\" => {\n            let command = json::text_at(\n                json::member_at(&request, \"\", \"command\")?,\n                \"command\",\n                \"a command name\",\n            )?\n            .to_owned();\n            let input = request\n                .member(\"input\")\n                .cloned()\n                .unwrap_or(json::Value::Object(Vec::new()));\n            let outcome = with_system(|system| system.run(&command, &input))?;\n            json::member(out, \"command\");\n            json::push_text(out, &command);\n            json::member(out, \"outcome\");\n            out.push_str(&outcome);\n            observe(out);\n        }\n        \"redeliver\" => {\n            let occurrence = json::integer_at(\n                json::member_at(&request, \"\", \"occurrence\")?,\n                \"occurrence\",\n                \"an occurrence index\",\n            )?;\n            let occurrence = usize::try_from(occurrence)\n                .map_err(|_| BridgeError::NoSuchOccurrence(usize::MAX))?;\n            with_system(|system| system.replay(occurrence))?;\n            observe(out);\n        }\n        other => return Err(BridgeError::UnknownRequest(other.to_owned())),\n    }\n    out.push('}');\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// The whole observable surface, written into an answer already in progress.\nfn observe(out: &mut String) {\n    let (log, invoked, projected) =\n        with_system(|system| (system.log(), system.invoked(), system.projected()));\n    json::member(out, \"log\");\n    out.push_str(&log);\n    json::member(out, \"invocations\");\n    out.push_str(&invoked);\n    json::member(out, \"views\");\n    out.push_str(&projected);\n}\n\n// ---- the boundary --------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n//\n// Three exports and no code generation on either side. A caller reserves a buffer of the request's\n// byte length, writes UTF-8 into the module's memory at the address it gets back, calls\n// `ess_dispatch`, and reads `ess_output_len` bytes from the address that returns. The buffers are\n// ordinary `Vec<u8>` and `String` this module owns; nothing here dereferences a raw pointer.\n\n/// Reserves a buffer of `length` bytes for the next request and answers its address.\n///\n/// The buffer is zeroed and owned by this module until the next reservation, so a caller may write\n/// into it and then call [`ess_dispatch`]. Reserving again discards whatever was there.\n#[no_mangle]\npub extern \"C\" fn ess_input_reserve(length: u32) -> u32 {\n    INPUT.with(|held| {\n        let mut held = held.borrow_mut();\n        *held = vec![0; length as usize];\n        held.as_ptr() as usize as u32\n    })\n}\n\n/// Serves the request in the reserved buffer and answers the address of the JSON response.\n///\n/// Its length is [`ess_output_len`]. The response stays valid until the next dispatch.\n#[no_mangle]\npub extern \"C\" fn ess_dispatch() -> u32 {\n    let request = INPUT.with(|held| String::from_utf8_lossy(&held.borrow()).into_owned());\n    let response = serve(&request);\n    OUTPUT.with(|held| {\n        let mut held = held.borrow_mut();\n        *held = response;\n        held.as_ptr() as usize as u32\n    })\n}\n\n/// The length in bytes of the response [`ess_dispatch`] last produced.\n#[no_mangle]\npub extern \"C\" fn ess_output_len() -> u32 {\n    OUTPUT.with(|held| held.borrow().len() as u32)\n}\n",

      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },
  "crates/demo-web/src/wire.rs": {
    "path": "crates/demo-web/src/wire.rs",
    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n// contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n\n//! Every generated declaration, as JSON, in the renderings the published wire contracts fix.\n//!\n//! Generated from the model beside the types it crosses, so a field renamed in the specification\n//! is renamed here in the same regeneration. An absent optional field is omitted rather\n//! than sent as `null`, which is what the `required` list of the published schema says.\n\nuse crate::json;\n\n/// Writes the event `demo.core.Fired` as JSON.\npub fn encode_event_demo_core_fired(_value: &demo_types::core::Fired, out: &mut String) {\n    out.push('{');\n    out.push('}');\n}\n\n/// Writes the input of `demo.core.Fire` as JSON.\npub fn encode_command_demo_core_fire(_value: &demo_types::core::Fire, out: &mut String) {\n    out.push('{');\n    out.push('}');\n}\n\n/// Reads the input of `demo.core.Fire` from JSON.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`json::DecodeError`] naming the path and what the declaration says belongs there.\npub fn decode_command_demo_core_fire(_value: &json::Value, _at: &str) -> Result<demo_types::core::Fire, json::DecodeError> {\n    Ok(demo_types::core::Fire {\n    })\n}\n\n/// Writes the outcome of `demo.core.Fire` as JSON: the branch taken, what it published, and the declared\n/// refusal it carries where it carries one.\npub fn encode_outcome_demo_core_fire(value: &demo_types::core::FireOutcome, out: &mut String) {\n    out.push('{');\n    match value {\n        demo_types::core::FireOutcome::Done { fired } => {\n            json::member(out, \"outcome\");\n            json::push_text(out, \"done\");\n            json::member(out, \"published\");\n            out.push('[');\n            out.push('{');\n            json::member(out, \"event\");\n            json::push_text(out, \"demo.core.Fired\");\n            json::member(out, \"payload\");\n            encode_event_demo_core_fired(fired, out);\n            out.push('}');\n            out.push(']');\n        }\n    }\n    out.push('}');\n}\n",

      "of": "whole-model"

  "index.html": {
    "path": "index.html",
    "contents": "<!--\n  generated from demo v1\n  model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n  contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n  do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n-->\n<!doctype html>\n<html lang=\"en\">\n<head>\n<meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n<meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1\">\n<title>demo v1 — a synthesised system</title>\n<style>\n:root { color-scheme: light dark; --edge: color-mix(in srgb, currentColor 18%, transparent); }\n* { box-sizing: border-box; }\nbody { margin: 0 auto; padding: 2rem 1.5rem 6rem; max-width: 62rem; font: 15px/1.55 ui-sans-serif, system-ui, sans-serif; }\nheader { border-bottom: 1px solid var(--edge); padding-bottom: 1.5rem; margin-bottom: 1rem; }\nh1 { font-size: 1.6rem; margin: 0 0 .25rem; }\nh2 { font-size: 1.1rem; margin: 2.5rem 0 .5rem; letter-spacing: .04em; text-transform: uppercase; opacity: .75; }\nh3 { font-size: 1rem; margin: 0 0 .2rem; }\np { margin: .4rem 0; }\ncode, pre { font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; font-size: .85em; }\ncode.name { opacity: .6; }\ncode.spelling { opacity: .55; margin-left: .4rem; }\ncode.value { white-space: pre-wrap; word-break: break-word; }\n.note { opacity: .7; font-size: .9em; }\n.contract { border-left: 2px solid var(--edge); padding-left: .75rem; opacity: .85; }\n.card { border: 1px solid var(--edge); border-radius: 8px; padding: 1rem 1.1rem; margin: .75rem 0; }\n.record { display: grid; gap: .6rem; margin: .75rem 0; }\n.record label { display: grid; gap: .15rem; }\n.record label.inline, label.inline { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: .4rem; }\n.field { font-weight: 600; }\n.nested { border-left: 2px solid var(--edge); padding-left: .75rem; margin-top: .3rem; }\n.row { display: flex; gap: .4rem; align-items: start; margin-bottom: .3rem; }\ninput[type=\"text\"], input[type=\"number\"], select { padding: .35rem .5rem; border: 1px solid var(--edge); border-radius: 5px; background: transparent; color: inherit; font: inherit; width: 100%; }\n.row input { width: auto; flex: 1; }\nbutton { padding: .4rem .8rem; border: 1px solid var(--edge); border-radius: 5px; background: transparent; color: inherit; font: inherit; cursor: pointer; }\nbutton:hover { border-color: currentColor; }\ntable { border-collapse: collapse; width: 100%; margin: .5rem 0; }\nth, td { text-align: left; vertical-align: top; padding: .35rem .6rem .35rem 0; border-bottom: 1px solid var(--edge); }\nth { font-size: .78em; letter-spacing: .05em; text-transform: uppercase; opacity: .6; font-weight: 600; }\ndl { display: grid; grid-template-columns: max-content 1fr; gap: .1rem .9rem; margin: .75rem 0 0; font-size: .85em; }\ndt { opacity: .6; }\ndd { margin: 0; font-family: ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, monospace; word-break: break-all; }\ndetails { margin: .5rem 0; }\nsummary { cursor: pointer; opacity: .8; }\n.accepted strong { color: color-mix(in srgb, currentColor 40%, green); }\n.refused { color: color-mix(in srgb, currentColor 35%, crimson); }\n</style>\n</head>\n<body>\n<header>\n<h1 id=\"system\">demo v1</h1>\n<p id=\"summary\"></p>\n<dl id=\"provenance\"></dl>\n<p id=\"realization\" class=\"note\"></p>\n</header>\n<main>\n<section id=\"commands-panel\">\n<h2>Commands</h2>\n<p class=\"note\">Every command this specification declares, with the input it takes. Sending one runs the component's port and then pumps the transport until quiescent.</p>\n<div id=\"commands\"></div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"outcome-panel\">\n<h2>Last outcome</h2>\n<div id=\"outcome\"><p class=\"note\">Nothing sent yet.</p></div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"log-panel\">\n<h2>Event log</h2>\n<p class=\"note\">The system's observable record: everything any component published, in publication order. Redelivering an occurrence is the duplicate an <code>at least once</code> guarantee explicitly permits — the occurrence is not published again, and every binding that reacts to it runs again.</p>\n<div id=\"log\"></div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"invocations-panel\">\n<h2>Binding invocations</h2>\n<p class=\"note\">What each binding invoked, and the input it filled — the transport's own record.</p>\n<div id=\"invocations\"></div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"state-panel\">\n<h2>State</h2>\n<p class=\"note\">Instances are observable exactly as far as the model publishes them: through declared views. The lifecycle beside each entity is what the specification says may happen to one.</p>\n<div id=\"views\"></div>\n<div id=\"entities\"></div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"model-panel\">\n<h2>Model</h2>\n<div id=\"model\"></div>\n</section>\n</main>\n<script type=\"module\">\nimport { open } from \"./bridge.js\";\n\n// Where `cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown` leaves the module, and where a host\n// that links a realization leaves its own. Both answer this page: the exports travel from the\n// bridge's `rlib` into the host's `cdylib`.\nconst CANDIDATES = [\n  \"target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/demo_web.wasm\",\n  \"target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/debug/demo_web.wasm\",\n  \"demo_web.wasm\",\n];\n\nlet system = null;\nlet catalog = null;\n\n/** Loads the first module that answers, so a debug build and a release build both work. */\nasync function load() {\n  const failures = [];\n  for (const candidate of CANDIDATES) {\n    try {\n      const response = await fetch(candidate);\n      if (!response.ok) { failures.push(`${candidate}: ${response.status}`); continue; }\n      return { system: await open(await response.arrayBuffer()), from: candidate };\n    } catch (error) {\n      failures.push(`${candidate}: ${error.message}`);\n    }\n  }\n  throw new Error(`no module answered.\\n${failures.join(\"\\n\")}`);\n}\n\n/** One element, with attributes and children, because a page of `innerHTML` is a page of holes. */\nfunction element(tag, attributes = {}, children = []) {\n  const node = document.createElement(tag);\n  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(attributes)) {\n    if (key === \"class\") node.className = value;\n    else if (key === \"text\") node.textContent = value;\n    else node.setAttribute(key, value);\n  }\n  for (const child of [].concat(children)) {\n    node.appendChild(typeof child === \"string\" ? document.createTextNode(child) : child);\n  }\n  return node;\n}\n\n/** A table with a header row and one row per record. */\nfunction table(columns, rows) {\n  const head = element(\"tr\", {}, columns.map((column) => element(\"th\", { text: column })));\n  const body = rows.map((cells) => element(\"tr\", {}, cells.map((cell) =>\n    element(\"td\", {}, typeof cell === \"string\" ? [cell] : [cell]))));\n  return element(\"table\", {}, [element(\"thead\", {}, [head]), element(\"tbody\", {}, body)]);\n}\n\n/** A value as compact JSON, which is what it is. */\nfunction json(value) {\n  return element(\"code\", { class: \"value\", text: JSON.stringify(value) });\n}\n\n// ---- input controls, built from the declared type ------------------------------------------------\n\n/** A control for one declared type reference: `{ node, read }`. `read()` may answer undefined. */\nfunction control(type) {\n  switch (type.kind) {\n    case \"primitive\": return primitive(type.name);\n    case \"declared\": return declared(type.name);\n    case \"optional\": return optional(type.of);\n    case \"list\": return list(type.of);\n    case \"map\": return map(type);\n    default: return primitive(\"string\");\n  }\n}\n\nfunction primitive(name) {\n  if (name === \"boolean\") {\n    const node = element(\"input\", { type: \"checkbox\" });\n    return { node, read: () => node.checked };\n  }\n  if (name === \"integer\") {\n    const node = element(\"input\", { type: \"number\", step: \"1\", value: \"0\" });\n    return { node, read: () => Number.parseInt(node.value === \"\" ? \"0\" : node.value, 10) };\n  }\n  const hints = {\n    decimal: \"10.50\",\n    timestamp: \"2026-01-01T00:00:00Z\",\n    duration: \"P30D\",\n    uuid: \"00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000000\",\n    bytes: \"base64\",\n  };\n  const node = element(\"input\", { type: \"text\", placeholder: hints[name] ?? \"\" });\n  return { node, read: () => node.value };\n}\n\nfunction declared(name) {\n  const declaration = catalog.types[name];\n  if (!declaration) return primitive(\"string\");\n  if (declaration.kind === \"newtype\") return control(declaration.of);\n  if (declaration.kind === \"enum\") {\n    const node = element(\"select\", {}, declaration.variants.map((variant) =>\n      element(\"option\", { value: variant, text: variant })));\n    return { node, read: () => node.value };\n  }\n  if (declaration.kind === \"struct\") {\n    return record(declaration.fields);\n  }\n  const labels = Object.keys(declaration.variants);\n  const choice = element(\"select\", {}, labels.map((label) =>\n    element(\"option\", { value: label, text: label })));\n  const holder = element(\"div\", { class: \"nested\" });\n  let held = null;\n  const show = () => {\n    holder.replaceChildren();\n    held = control(declaration.variants[choice.value].type);\n    holder.appendChild(held.node);\n  };\n  choice.addEventListener(\"change\", show);\n  show();\n  const node = element(\"div\", { class: \"union\" }, [choice, holder]);\n  return {\n    node,\n    read: () => {\n      const value = { [declaration.tag]: choice.value };\n      const payload = held.read();\n      if (payload !== undefined) value[declaration.content] = payload;\n      return value;\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n/** A group of declared fields, each labelled with its own name and type. */\nfunction record(fields) {\n  const parts = fields.map((field) => {\n    const held = control(field.type);\n    const label = element(\"label\", {}, [\n      element(\"span\", { class: \"field\", text: field.display ?? field.name }),\n      element(\"code\", { class: \"spelling\", text: field.spelling }),\n      held.node,\n    ]);\n    if (field.summary) label.appendChild(element(\"small\", { text: field.summary }));\n    return { field, held, label };\n  });\n  const node = element(\"div\", { class: \"record\" }, parts.map((part) => part.label));\n  return {\n    node,\n    read: () => {\n      const value = {};\n      for (const part of parts) {\n        const held = part.held.read();\n        if (held !== undefined) value[part.field.wire] = held;\n      }\n      return value;\n    },\n  };\n}\n\nfunction optional(of) {\n  const present = element(\"input\", { type: \"checkbox\" });\n  const holder = element(\"div\", { class: \"nested\" });\n  const held = control(of);\n  holder.appendChild(held.node);\n  const sync = () => { holder.hidden = !present.checked; };\n  present.addEventListener(\"change\", sync);\n  sync();\n  const node = element(\"div\", { class: \"optional\" }, [\n    element(\"label\", { class: \"inline\" }, [present, element(\"span\", { text: \"present\" })]),\n    holder,\n  ]);\n  return { node, read: () => (present.checked ? held.read() : undefined) };\n}\n\nfunction list(of) {\n  const rows = [];\n  const holder = element(\"div\", { class: \"nested\" });\n  const add = element(\"button\", { type: \"button\", text: \"add\" });\n  add.addEventListener(\"click\", () => {\n    const held = control(of);\n    const remove = element(\"button\", { type: \"button\", text: \"remove\" });\n    const row = element(\"div\", { class: \"row\" }, [held.node, remove]);\n    remove.addEventListener(\"click\", () => {\n      holder.removeChild(row);\n      rows.splice(rows.indexOf(entry), 1);\n    });\n    const entry = { held, row };\n    rows.push(entry);\n    holder.appendChild(row);\n  });\n  const node = element(\"div\", { class: \"list\" }, [holder, add]);\n  return { node, read: () => rows.map((entry) => entry.held.read()) };\n}\n\nfunction map(type) {\n  const rows = [];\n  const holder = element(\"div\", { class: \"nested\" });\n  const add = element(\"button\", { type: \"button\", text: \"add\" });\n  add.addEventListener(\"click\", () => {\n    const key = element(\"input\", { type: \"text\", placeholder: type.key });\n    const held = control(type.value);\n    const remove = element(\"button\", { type: \"button\", text: \"remove\" });\n    const row = element(\"div\", { class: \"row\" }, [key, held.node, remove]);\n    remove.addEventListener(\"click\", () => {\n      holder.removeChild(row);\n      rows.splice(rows.indexOf(entry), 1);\n    });\n    const entry = { key, held, row };\n    rows.push(entry);\n    holder.appendChild(row);\n  });\n  const node = element(\"div\", { class: \"map\" }, [holder, add]);\n  return {\n    node,\n    read: () => {\n      const value = {};\n      for (const entry of rows) value[entry.key.value] = entry.held.read();\n      return value;\n    },\n  };\n}\n\n// ---- rendering ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nfunction renderHeader(from, realized) {\n  document.getElementById(\"system\").textContent = `${catalog.display} ${catalog.version}`;\n  document.getElementById(\"summary\").textContent = catalog.summary ?? \"\";\n  const provenance = document.getElementById(\"provenance\");\n  provenance.replaceChildren();\n  const facts = [\n    [\"system\", catalog.system],\n    [\"specification\", catalog.provenance.specification_version],\n    [\"model digest\", catalog.provenance.source_digest],\n    [\"contract digest\", catalog.provenance.contract_digest],\n    [\"plan\", `${catalog.plan.generated} generated · ${catalog.plan.obligations} obligations · ${catalog.plan.refused} refused`],\n    [\"module\", from],\n  ];\n  for (const [term, definition] of facts) {\n    provenance.appendChild(element(\"dt\", { text: term }));\n    provenance.appendChild(element(\"dd\", { text: definition }));\n  }\n  document.getElementById(\"realization\").textContent = realized\n    ? \"A realization is installed: the obligations below are implemented.\"\n    : \"No realization is installed. Every command will answer with the typed refusal naming what the plan owes — that is this tree's honest empty state.\";\n}\n\nfunction renderCommands() {\n  const holder = document.getElementById(\"commands\");\n  holder.replaceChildren();\n  for (const command of catalog.commands) {\n    const card = element(\"article\", { class: \"card\" });\n    card.appendChild(element(\"h3\", { text: command.display }));\n    card.appendChild(element(\"code\", { class: \"name\", text: command.name }));\n    if (command.summary) card.appendChild(element(\"p\", { text: command.summary }));\n    card.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text: command.component\n      ? `accepted by ${command.component}`\n      : `not dispatchable: ${command.refusal}` }));\n    if (command.behavior && command.behavior.disposition === \"obligation\") {\n      card.appendChild(element(\"details\", {}, [\n        element(\"summary\", { text: \"the behaviour is an obligation\" }),\n        element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text: command.behavior.why }),\n        element(\"p\", { class: \"contract\", text: command.behavior.contract }),\n      ]));\n    }\n    const outcomes = command.outcomes.map((outcome) => [\n      element(\"code\", { text: outcome.name }),\n      outcome.condition,\n      outcome.publishes.length ? outcome.publishes.join(\", \") : \"—\",\n      outcome.error ?? \"—\",\n    ]);\n    card.appendChild(element(\"details\", {}, [\n      element(\"summary\", { text: `${command.outcomes.length} declared outcome(s)` }),\n      table([\"outcome\", \"when\", \"publishes\", \"refuses with\"], outcomes),\n    ]));\n    if (command.dispatchable) {\n      const held = record(command.input);\n      const send = element(\"button\", { type: \"button\", text: `send ${command.display}` });\n      send.addEventListener(\"click\", () => dispatch(command.name, held.read()));\n      card.appendChild(held.node);\n      card.appendChild(send);\n    }\n    holder.appendChild(card);\n  }\n}\n\nfunction renderOutcome(answer) {\n  const holder = document.getElementById(\"outcome\");\n  holder.replaceChildren();\n  if (!answer) { holder.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text: \"Nothing sent yet.\" })); return; }\n  if (answer.ok === false) {\n    holder.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"refused\", text: `refused — ${answer.error.kind}` }));\n    holder.appendChild(json(answer.error));\n    return;\n  }\n  const outcome = answer.outcome;\n  if (!outcome) { holder.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text: \"Delivered again.\" })); return; }\n  holder.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"accepted\" }, [\n    element(\"code\", { text: answer.command }),\n    ` took the declared outcome `,\n    element(\"strong\", { text: outcome.outcome }),\n  ]));\n  if (outcome.refusal) {\n    holder.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"refused\", text: `refused with ${outcome.refusal.error}` }));\n    holder.appendChild(json(outcome.refusal.payload));\n  }\n  if (outcome.published && outcome.published.length) {\n    holder.appendChild(table([\"published\", \"payload\"],\n      outcome.published.map((entry) => [element(\"code\", { text: entry.event }), json(entry.payload)])));\n  }\n}\n\nfunction renderLog(log) {\n  const holder = document.getElementById(\"log\");\n  holder.replaceChildren();\n  if (!log.length) { holder.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text: \"Nothing published yet.\" })); return; }\n  holder.appendChild(table([\"#\", \"event\", \"payload\", \"\"], log.map((entry) => {\n    const again = element(\"button\", { type: \"button\", text: \"redeliver\" });\n    again.addEventListener(\"click\", () => redeliver(entry.occurrence));\n    return [String(entry.occurrence), element(\"code\", { text: entry.event }), json(entry.payload), again];\n  })));\n}\n\nfunction renderInvocations(invocations) {\n  const holder = document.getElementById(\"invocations\");\n  holder.replaceChildren();\n  if (!invocations.length) { holder.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text: \"No binding has invoked anything yet.\" })); return; }\n  holder.appendChild(table([\"binding\", \"reacting to\", \"invoked\", \"input\"], invocations.map((entry) => [\n    element(\"code\", { text: entry.binding }),\n    element(\"code\", { text: entry.event }),\n    element(\"code\", { text: entry.command }),\n    json(entry.input),\n  ])));\n}\n\nfunction renderViews(views) {\n  const holder = document.getElementById(\"views\");\n  holder.replaceChildren();\n  for (const view of catalog.views) {\n    const card = element(\"article\", { class: \"card\" });\n    card.appendChild(element(\"h3\", { text: view.display }));\n    card.appendChild(element(\"code\", { class: \"name\", text: view.name }));\n    card.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text:\n      `projects ${view.entity} at ${view.consistency} consistency` + (view.filter ? `, where ${view.filter}` : \"\") }));\n    const served = views[view.name];\n    if (served && served.unmet) {\n      card.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"refused\", text:\n        `unmet obligation — ${served.unmet.capability} “${served.unmet.source}”. Nothing has implemented this projection.` }));\n    } else if (served && served.rows.length) {\n      card.appendChild(table(view.fields.map((field) => field.display ?? field.name),\n        served.rows.map((row) => view.fields.map((field) => json(row[field.wire])))));\n    } else {\n      card.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text: \"no rows\" }));\n    }\n    holder.appendChild(card);\n  }\n}\n\nfunction renderEntities() {\n  const holder = document.getElementById(\"entities\");\n  holder.replaceChildren();\n  for (const entity of catalog.entities) {\n    const card = element(\"article\", { class: \"card\" });\n    card.appendChild(element(\"h3\", { text: `${entity.display} — lifecycle` }));\n    card.appendChild(element(\"code\", { class: \"name\", text: entity.name }));\n    card.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text:\n      `starts in ${entity.initial}` + (entity.terminal.length ? `; may rest in ${entity.terminal.join(\", \")}` : \"\") }));\n    card.appendChild(table([\"move\", \"from\", \"to\"],\n      entity.transitions.map((move) => [element(\"code\", { text: move.name }), move.from.join(\", \"), move.to])));\n    if (entity.views.length) {\n      card.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text: `observable through ${entity.views.join(\", \")}` }));\n    } else {\n      card.appendChild(element(\"p\", { class: \"note\", text: \"no declared view projects this entity, so its instances are not observable from here\" }));\n    }\n    holder.appendChild(card);\n  }\n}\n\nfunction renderModel() {\n  const holder = document.getElementById(\"model\");\n  holder.replaceChildren();\n  holder.appendChild(element(\"details\", {}, [\n    element(\"summary\", { text: `${catalog.components.length} component(s)` }),\n    table([\"component\", \"accepts\", \"publishes\"], catalog.components.map((component) => [\n      element(\"code\", { text: component.name }), component.accepts.join(\", \"), component.publishes.join(\", \"),\n    ])),\n  ]));\n  holder.appendChild(element(\"details\", {}, [\n    element(\"summary\", { text: `${catalog.bindings.length} binding(s)` }),\n    table([\"binding\", \"on\", \"invokes\", \"delivery\", \"on failure\"], catalog.bindings.map((binding) => [\n      element(\"code\", { text: binding.name }),\n      element(\"code\", { text: binding.event }),\n      element(\"code\", { text: binding.command }),\n      binding.delivery,\n      binding.on_failure + (binding.escalation ? ` into ${binding.escalation}` : \"\"),\n    ])),\n  ]));\n  holder.appendChild(element(\"details\", {}, [\n    element(\"summary\", { text: `${catalog.conversions.length} declared crossing(s)` }),\n    table([\"from\", \"to\", \"because\"], catalog.conversions.map((conversion) => [\n      element(\"code\", { text: conversion.from }), element(\"code\", { text: conversion.to }), conversion.because,\n    ])),\n  ]));\n  holder.appendChild(element(\"details\", {}, [\n    element(\"summary\", { text: `${catalog.events.length} event(s)` }),\n    table([\"event\", \"carries\"], catalog.events.map((event) => [\n      element(\"code\", { text: event.name }),\n      event.fields.map((field) => `${field.name}: ${field.spelling}`).join(\", \") || \"—\",\n    ])),\n  ]));\n}\n\n// ---- talking to the system ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nfunction observe(answer) {\n  renderOutcome(answer);\n  if (answer.log) renderLog(answer.log);\n  if (answer.invocations) renderInvocations(answer.invocations);\n  if (answer.views) renderViews(answer.views);\n}\n\nfunction dispatch(command, input) {\n  observe(system.request({ request: \"command\", command, input }));\n}\n\nfunction redeliver(occurrence) {\n  observe(system.request({ request: \"redeliver\", occurrence }));\n}\n\nasync function main() {\n  let opened;\n  try {\n    opened = await load();\n  } catch (error) {\n    document.getElementById(\"realization\").textContent = String(error.message);\n    return;\n  }\n  system = opened.system;\n  const answer = system.request({ request: \"catalog\" });\n  catalog = answer.catalog;\n  renderHeader(opened.from, system.realized);\n  renderCommands();\n  renderEntities();\n  renderModel();\n  observe(system.request({ request: \"observe\" }));\n}\n\nmain();\n\n</script>\n</body>\n</html>\n",

  "README.md": {
    "path": "README.md",
    "contents": "<!--\n  generated from demo v1\n  model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n  contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n  do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n-->\n# demo in a browser\n\n**Do not edit these files.** They are synthesised from `examples/demo/` by `cargo xtask synth`, and CI fails if they differ from what the specification determines, if the module stops building for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`, or if `index.html` calls an export the module does not have. Regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`.\n\nWhat is here:\n\n| file | what it is |\n| --- | --- |\n| `index.html` | the page. Every command, field, event, view and state on it is built from `catalog.json`; none of it is typed into the HTML |\n| `bridge.js` | the glue: reserve a buffer, write the request, call the module, read the response |\n| `catalog.json` | the model, as the page reads it — and the same bytes the module carries |\n| `crates/demo-web/` | the bridge crate: JSON in, JSON out, and the exports a browser calls |\n| `PLAN.md`, `plan.json` | the plan, byte-identical to every other target's |\n| `TARGET.md`, `target.json` | what a browser could not carry across the plan |\n\n## Building it\n\n```console\n$ rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown\n$ cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown\n```\n\nThat produces `target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/demo_web.wasm`. Every command will answer with the typed refusal naming an unmet obligation, because this tree implements none of them — that is the honest empty state, and the page shows the plan's own contract beside each one.\n\n## Running it against a realization\n\nA host crate that depends on this one as an `rlib`, links an implementation of every obligation, and exports `ess_realize` hands the assembled system to `install`. The exports below travel into that host's `cdylib`, so the same page drives it, and the page calls `ess_realize` if it is there. `examples/demo-web/` in this repository is that host.\n\nThe page looks for its module in three places, in order: the release build beside this file, the debug build, and `demo_web.wasm` in this directory. That last one is how a *realized* module is opened — copy it in under that name:\n\n```console\n$ (cd ../../../examples/demo-web && cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown)\n$ cp ../../../examples/demo-web/target/wasm32-unknown-unknown/release/*.wasm ./demo_web.wasm\n```\n\nThen serve this directory and open `index.html` — a browser will not instantiate WebAssembly from a `file://` URL:\n\n```console\n$ python3 -m http.server\n$ open http://localhost:8000/index.html\n```\n\n## The exports\n\n| export | what it does |\n| --- | --- |\n| `ess_input_reserve` | reserves a buffer of the given length for the next request and answers its address in linear memory |\n| `ess_dispatch` | serves the request in that buffer and answers the address of the JSON response |\n| `ess_output_len` | the length in bytes of the response just produced |\n| `ess_realize` | optional, and never in this module: a host that links a realization exports it, and the page calls it if it is there |\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },
