JSON at this system's boundary: a reader, a writer, and the base64 codec `Bytes` needs.\n//!\n//! Written here rather than taken from a crate because this workspace has no dependencies: it is\n//! built inside a gate that reaches no network. The surface is exactly what the generated `wire`\n//! module beside it uses — nothing general, nothing speculative.\n\nuse std::fmt;\n\n/// How deep a document from a caller may nest before it is refused.\n///\n/// A caller can send anything, and a recursive reader with no limit turns a hostile document\n/// into a stack overflow — which is a crash the process cannot catch and cannot report. Sixty-four is far past any model this emitter can produce: the specification's own\n/// type references are refused past thirty-two.\nconst DEPTH: usize = 64;\n\n/// A JSON value, as it arrived.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]\npub enum Value {\n    /// `null`.\n    Null,\n    /// `true` or `false`.\n    Bool(bool),\n    /// A number, kept in the spelling it arrived in rather than parsed into a float.\n    ///\n    /// A float would round a decimal the model deliberately carries as text, and this reader has\n    /// no business deciding that `10.50` is `10.5`.\n    Number(String),\n    /// A string, with its escapes resolved.\n    Text(String),\n    /// An array, in order.\n    Array(Vec<Value>),\n    /// An object, in the order its members arrived.\n    ///\n    /// A list of pairs rather than a map: order is what makes a rendering reproducible, and a\n    /// duplicate key is a fact about the document rather than something to silently collapse.\n    Object(Vec<(String, Value)>),\n}\n\nimpl Value {\n    /// The member with this name, or `None` — including when this is not an object at all.\n    pub fn member(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&Self> {\n        match self {\n            Self::Object(members) => members\n                .iter()\n                .find(|(key, _)| key == name)\n                .map(|(_, value)| value),\n            _ => None,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// What this value is, in one word, for a message about what was expected instead.\n    pub fn describes(&self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            Self::Null => \"null\",\n            Self::Bool(_) => \"a boolean\",\n            Self::Number(_) => \"a number\",\n            Self::Text(_) => \"a string\",\n            Self::Array(_) => \"an array\",\n            Self::Object(_) => \"an object\",\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// A document the reader refused, and where.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct ParseError {\n    /// The byte offset it stopped at.\n    pub at: usize,\n    /// What would have been legal there.\n    pub expected: String,\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for ParseError {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        write!(f, \"at byte {}: expected {}\", self.at, self.expected)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads one JSON document.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`ParseError`] naming the offset and what was expected there, for anything that is not exactly\n/// one well-formed value followed by whitespace.\npub fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    let bytes = text.as_bytes();\n    let mut at = 0;\n    skip(bytes, &mut at);\n    let value = read(bytes, &mut at, 0)?;\n    skip(bytes, &mut at);\n    if at != bytes.len() {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at,\n            expected: \"the end of the document\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    }\n    Ok(value)\n}\n\n/// Advances past whitespace.\nfn skip(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) {\n    while let Some(byte) = bytes.get(*at) {\n        if matches!(byte, b' ' | b'\\t' | b'\\n' | b'\\r') {\n            *at += 1;\n        } else {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads one value at `at`, which is already past any leading whitespace.\nfn read(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize, depth: usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    if depth > DEPTH {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"a document nested less than 64 deep\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    }\n    match bytes.get(*at) {\n        Some(b'{') => object(bytes, at, depth),\n        Some(b'[') => array(bytes, at, depth),\n        Some(b'\"') => text(bytes, at).map(Value::Text),\n        Some(b't') => literal(bytes, at, b\"true\", Value::Bool(true)),\n        Some(b'f') => literal(bytes, at, b\"false\", Value::Bool(false)),\n        Some(b'n') => literal(bytes, at, b\"null\", Value::Null),\n        Some(byte) if *byte == b'-' || byte.is_ascii_digit() => number(bytes, at),\n        _ => Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"a value\".to_owned(),\n        }),\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads one of the three bare words.\nfn literal(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize, word: &[u8], value: Value) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    if bytes.len() < *at + word.len() || &bytes[*at..*at + word.len()] != word {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: format!(\"`{}`\", String::from_utf8_lossy(word)),\n        });\n    }\n    *at += word.len();\n    Ok(value)\n}\n\n/// Reads a number, keeping its spelling.\nfn number(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    let start = *at;\n    if bytes.get(*at) == Some(&b'-') {\n        *at += 1;\n    }\n    let digits = *at;\n    while bytes.get(*at).is_some_and(u8::is_ascii_digit) {\n        *at += 1;\n    }\n    if *at == digits {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"a digit\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    }\n    if bytes.get(*at) == Some(&b'.') {\n        *at += 1;\n        let fraction = *at;\n        while bytes.get(*at).is_some_and(u8::is_ascii_digit) {\n            *at += 1;\n        }\n        if *at == fraction {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"a digit after the decimal point\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n    }\n    if matches!(bytes.get(*at), Some(b'e' | b'E')) {\n        *at += 1;\n        if matches!(bytes.get(*at), Some(b'+' | b'-')) {\n            *at += 1;\n        }\n        let exponent = *at;\n        while bytes.get(*at).is_some_and(u8::is_ascii_digit) {\n            *at += 1;\n        }\n        if *at == exponent {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"a digit in the exponent\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(Value::Number(\n        String::from_utf8_lossy(&bytes[start..*at]).into_owned(),\n    ))\n}\n\n/// Reads a string, resolving its escapes.\nfn text(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<String, ParseError> {\n    *at += 1;\n    let mut out = String::new();\n    loop {\n        let Some(byte) = bytes.get(*at).copied() else {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"a closing quote\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        };\n        match byte {\n            b'\"' => {\n                *at += 1;\n                return Ok(out);\n            }\n            b'\\\\' => {\n                *at += 1;\n                let escaped = escape(bytes, at)?;\n                out.push(escaped);\n            }\n            _ => {\n                let start = *at;\n                while bytes\n                    .get(*at)\n                    .is_some_and(|byte| *byte != b'\"' && *byte != b'\\\\')\n                {\n                    *at += 1;\n                }\n                out.push_str(&String::from_utf8_lossy(&bytes[start..*at]));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads one escape sequence, the backslash already consumed.\nfn escape(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<char, ParseError> {\n    let Some(byte) = bytes.get(*at).copied() else {\n        return Err(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"an escape\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    };\n    *at += 1;\n    Ok(match byte {\n        b'\"' => '\"',\n        b'\\\\' => '\\\\',\n        b'/' => '/',\n        b'b' => '\\u{8}',\n        b'f' => '\\u{c}',\n        b'n' => '\\n',\n        b'r' => '\\r',\n        b't' => '\\t',\n        b'u' => return unicode(bytes, at),\n        _ => {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"one of \\\" \\\\ / b f n r t u\".to_owned(),\n            })\n        }\n    })\n}\n\n/// Reads a `\\u` escape, joining a surrogate pair when it finds one.\nfn unicode(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<char, ParseError> {\n    let first = hex(bytes, at)?;\n    if (0xD800..0xDC00).contains(&first) {\n        // A lone high surrogate is not a character; the low half has to follow, escaped the same\n        // way. Refusing here rather than substituting a replacement character keeps a truncated\n        // document a refusal instead of a value nobody sent.\n        if bytes.get(*at) != Some(&b'\\\\') || bytes.get(*at + 1) != Some(&b'u') {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"the low half of a surrogate pair\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n        *at += 2;\n        let second = hex(bytes, at)?;\n        if !(0xDC00..0xE000).contains(&second) {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"the low half of a surrogate pair\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n        let joined = 0x1_0000 + ((first - 0xD800) << 10) + (second - 0xDC00);\n        return char::from_u32(joined).ok_or(ParseError {\n            at: *at,\n            expected: \"a character\".to_owned(),\n        });\n    }\n    char::from_u32(first).ok_or(ParseError {\n        at: *at,\n        expected: \"a character\".to_owned(),\n    })\n}\n\n/// Reads exactly four hexadecimal digits.\nfn hex(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize) -> Result<u32, ParseError> {\n    let mut value = 0;\n    for _ in 0..4 {\n        let Some(byte) = bytes.get(*at).copied() else {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"four hexadecimal digits\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        };\n        let digit = match byte {\n            b'0'..=b'9' => u32::from(byte - b'0'),\n            b'a'..=b'f' => u32::from(byte - b'a') + 10,\n digits\".to_owned(),\n                })\n            }\n        };\n        value = value * 16 + digit;\n        *at += 1;\n    }\n    Ok(value)\n}\n\n/// Reads an array.\nfn array(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize, depth: usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    *at += 1;\n    let mut items = Vec::new();\n    skip(bytes, at);\n    if bytes.get(*at) == Some(&b']') {\n        *at += 1;\n        return Ok(Value::Array(items));\n    }\n    loop {\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        items.push(read(bytes, at, depth + 1)?);\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        match bytes.get(*at) {\n            Some(b',') => *at += 1,\n            Some(b']') => {\n                *at += 1;\n                return Ok(Value::Array(items));\n            }\n            _ => {\n                return Err(ParseError {\n                    at: *at,\n                    expected: \"`,` or `]`\".to_owned(),\n                })\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Reads an object.\nfn object(bytes: &[u8], at: &mut usize, depth: usize) -> Result<Value, ParseError> {\n    *at += 1;\n    let mut members = Vec::new();\n    skip(bytes, at);\n    if bytes.get(*at) == Some(&b'}') {\n        *at += 1;\n        return Ok(Value::Object(members));\n    }\n    loop {\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        if bytes.get(*at) != Some(&b'\"') {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"a member name\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n        let name = text(bytes, at)?;\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        if bytes.get(*at) != Some(&b':') {\n            return Err(ParseError {\n                at: *at,\n                expected: \"`:`\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        }\n        *at += 1;\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        members.push((name, read(bytes, at, depth + 1)?));\n        skip(bytes, at);\n        match bytes.get(*at) {\n            Some(b',') => *at += 1,\n            Some(b'}') => {\n                *at += 1;\n                return Ok(Value::Object(members));\n            }\n            _ => {\n                return Err(ParseError {\n                    at: *at,\n                    expected: \"`,` or `}`\".to_owned(),\n                })\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// ---- writing -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// Appends a JSON string, escaped.\npub fn push_text(out: &mut String, text: &str) {\n    out.push('\"');\n    for character in text.chars() {\n        match character {\n            '\"' => out.push_str(\"\\\\\\\"\"),\n            '\\\\' => out.push_str(\"\\\\\\\\\"),\n            '\\n' => out.push_str(\"\\\\n\"),\n            '\\r' => out.push_str(\"\\\\r\"),\n            '\\t' => out.push_str(\"\\\\t\"),\n            // Every other control character has to be escaped for the document to be legal, and\n            // `\\u` is the only form JSON has for one.\n            character if (character as u32) < 0x20 => {\n                out.push_str(&format!(\"\\\\u{:04x}\", character as u32));\n            }\n            character => out.push(character),\n        }\n    }\n    out.push('\"');\n}\n\n/// Appends a JSON number.\npub fn push_integer(out: &mut String, value: i64) {\n    out.push_str(&value.to_string());\n}\n\n/// Appends `true` or `false`.\npub fn push_bool(out: &mut String, value: bool) {\n    out.push_str(if value { \"true\" } else { \"false\" });\n}\n\n/// Appends opaque bytes as the base64 string the published wire contracts fix.\npub fn push_base64(out: &mut String, bytes: &[u8]) {\n    let mut encoded = String::new();\n    for chunk in bytes.chunks(3) {\n        let a = u32::from(chunk[0]);\n        let b = chunk.get(1).copied().map_or(0, u32::from);\n        let c = chunk.get(2).copied().map_or(0, u32::from);\n        let packed = (a << 16) | (b << 8) | c;\n        encoded.push(ALPHABET[(packed >> 18) as usize & 63] as char);\n        encoded.push(ALPHABET[(packed >> 12) as usize & 63] as char);\n        encoded.push(if chunk.len() > 1 {\n            ALPHABET[(packed >> 6) as usize & 63] as char\n        } else {\n            '='\n        });\n        encoded.push(if chunk.len() > 2 {\n            ALPHABET[packed as usize & 63] as char\n        } else {\n            '='\n        });\n    }\n    push_text(out, &encoded);\n}\n\n/// The standard base64 alphabet, padded — the encoding the JSON Schema projection's pattern checks.\nconst ALPHABET: &[u8; 64] = b\"ABCDEFGHIJKLMNOPQRSTUVWXYZabcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz0123456789+/\";\n\n// ---- reading into the model's own shapes ---------------------------------------------------------\n\n/// A value that was not what the model's declaration says it is.\n///\n/// Carries the path it was reached at, because a caller sending a nested command input gets one\n/// message and \"expected a string\" without a path is a message nobody can act on.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct DecodeError {\n    /// Where in the document, as a dotted path from its root.\n    pub at: String,\n    /// What the declaration says belongs there.\n    pub expected: String,\n    /// What was there instead.\n    pub found: String,\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for DecodeError {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        write!(f, \"{}: expected {}, found {}\", self.at, self.expected, self.found)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl DecodeError {\n    /// A refusal at one path, with what was expected and what arrived.\n    pub fn of(at: &str, expected: &str, found: &Value) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            at: at.to_owned(),\n            expected: expected.to_owned(),\n            found: found.describes().to_owned(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// One step further into a document, for a message a reader can follow back.\npub fn nested(at: &str, step: &str) -> String {\n    if at.is_empty() {\n        step.to_owned()\n    } else {\n        format!(\"{at}.{step}\")\n    }\n}\n\n/// The string at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is anything else.\npub fn text_at<'a>(value: &'a Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<&'a str, DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Text(text) => Ok(text),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The integer at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is not a number, or is one no `Integer` can hold — a fraction,\n/// an exponent, or a magnitude past 64 bits. Refused rather than rounded: a silently truncated\n/// identifier is worse than a rejected command.\npub fn integer_at(value: &Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<i64, DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Number(number) => number.parse::<i64>().map_err(|_| DecodeError {\n            at: at.to_owned(),\n            expected: expected.to_owned(),\n            found: format!(\"the number {number}\"),\n        }),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The boolean at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is anything else.\npub fn bool_at(value: &Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<bool, DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Bool(flag) => Ok(*flag),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The bytes at this path, read from base64.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is not a string, or not base64.\npub fn bytes_at(value: &Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, DecodeError> {\n    let text = text_at(value, at, expected)?;\n    base64(text, at, expected)\n}\n\n/// Base64 text as bytes.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when a character is outside the alphabet.\nfn base64(text: &str, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, DecodeError> {\n    let mut packed = 0_u32;\n    let mut held = 0_u32;\n    let mut out = Vec::new();\n    for byte in text.bytes() {\n        if byte == b'=' {\n            break;\n        }\n        let Some(index) = ALPHABET.iter().position(|candidate| *candidate == byte) else {\n            return Err(DecodeError {\n                at: at.to_owned(),\n                expected: expected.to_owned(),\n                found: \"a string that is not base64\".to_owned(),\n            });\n        };\n        packed = (packed << 6) | index as u32;\n        held += 6;\n        if held >= 8 {\n            held -= 8;\n            out.push(((packed >> held) & 0xFF) as u8);\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(out)\n}\n\n/// The integer a map key spells.\n///\n/// JSON has only string keys, and the published wire contract constrains a non-string map's\n/// `propertyNames` to text of the declared primitive's shape. This is that constraint, applied\n/// where the value is actually built.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the key is not an integer's spelling.\npub fn key_integer(key: &str, at: &str) -> Result<i64, DecodeError> {\n    key.parse::<i64>().map_err(|_| DecodeError {\n        at: at.to_owned(),\n        expected: \"a key spelling an integer\".to_owned(),\n        found: format!(\"the key `{key}`\"),\n    })\n}\n\n/// The boolean a map key spells.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the key is neither `true` nor `false`.\npub fn key_bool(key: &str, at: &str) -> Result<bool, DecodeError> {\n    match key {\n        \"true\" => Ok(true),\n        \"false\" => Ok(false),\n        other => Err(DecodeError {\n            at: at.to_owned(),\n            expected: \"a key spelling `true` or `false`\".to_owned(),\n            found: format!(\"the key `{other}`\"),\n        }),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The bytes a base64 map key spells.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the key is not base64.\npub fn key_bytes(key: &str, at: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, DecodeError> {\n    base64(key, at, \"a base64 key\")\n}\n\n/// Opens a member of the object currently being written, with the separator it needs.\n///\n/// The separator is decided from the buffer rather than from a flag every generated writer would\n/// have to thread through every branch: an absent optional field is *omitted*, exactly as the\n/// published contract's `required` list says, and a comma written before nothing is a document no\n/// reader accepts. A complete JSON value never ends with `{`, so the buffer ending with one means\n/// this is the first member and nothing else.\npub fn member(out: &mut String, name: &str) {\n    if !out.ends_with('{') {\n        out.push(',');\n    }\n    push_text(out, name);\n    out.push(':');\n}\n\n/// The array at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is anything else.\npub fn items_at<'a>(value: &'a Value, at: &str, expected: &str) -> Result<&'a [Value], DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Array(items) => Ok(items),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The object members at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is anything else.\npub fn members_at<'a>(\n    value: &'a Value,\n    at: &str,\n    expected: &str,\n) -> Result<&'a [(String, Value)], DecodeError> {\n    match value {\n        Value::Object(members) => Ok(members),\n        other => Err(DecodeError::of(at, expected, other)),\n    }\n}\n\n/// The named member of the object at this path.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`DecodeError`] when the value is not an object, or the member is absent — absence of a\n/// required field is a refusal, never a default.\npub fn member_at<'a>(value: &'a Value, at: &str, name: &str) -> Result<&'a Value, DecodeError> {\n    let members = members_at(value, at, \"an object\")?;\n    members\n        .iter()\n        .find(|(key, _)| key == name)\n        .map(|(_, member)| member)\n        .ok_or_else(|| DecodeError {\n            at: nested(at, name),\n            expected: \"a value\".to_owned(),\n            found: \"nothing\".to_owned(),\n        })\n}\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },

    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 57d2eb2785ad2fb21ed2085027df2fe64d2c13ba96a7c2c117fa9ceb3a33bcb3\n// contract digest b13ddd0f4c243fbcbbce4d83a7b63a61419f752c65caa827b15acc1b3a3c99df\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize`\n\n//! The HTTP surface of `demo` v1, synthesised.\n//!\n//! One module per component the specification declares is reached over a network, each holding\n//! that component's route table, its listener and the two documents it publishes about itself.\n//! The routes are the ones the committed `OpenAPI` document declares, from the same mapping, so a\n//! path served here and a path published there cannot be two different answers.\n//!\n//! Generated, not written: the specification is the source of truth, and the door to changing\n//! anything here is `ess synthesize`. What is deliberately absent is absent by\n//! decision — no framework, no runtime, no second protocol, no concurrency, no authentication —\n//! and each absence is argued in the `TARGET.md` beside this workspace.\n\n#![forbid(unsafe_code)]\n#![deny(missing_docs)]\n\npub mod http;\npub mod json;\npub mod wire;\npub mod worker;\n",

  "crates/demo-server/src/wire.rs": {
    "path": "crates/demo-server/src/wire.rs",
    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 57d2eb2785ad2fb21ed2085027df2fe64d2c13ba96a7c2c117fa9ceb3a33bcb3\n// contract digest b13ddd0f4c243fbcbbce4d83a7b63a61419f752c65caa827b15acc1b3a3c99df\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize`\n//! Every generated declaration, as JSON, in the renderings the published wire contracts fix.\n//!\n//! Generated from the model beside the types it crosses, so a field renamed in the specification\n//! is renamed here in the same regeneration. An absent optional field is omitted rather\n//! than sent as `null`, which is what the `required` list of the published schema says.\n\nuse crate::json;\n\n/// Writes the event `demo.core.Out` as JSON.\npub fn encode_event_demo_core_out(_value: &demo_types::core::Out, out: &mut String) {\n    out.push('{');\n    out.push('}');\n}\n\n/// Writes the input of `demo.core.Fire` as JSON.\npub fn encode_command_demo_core_fire(_value: &demo_types::core::Fire, out: &mut String) {\n    out.push('{');\n    out.push('}');\n}\n\n/// Reads the input of `demo.core.Fire` from JSON.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// [`json::DecodeError`] naming the path and what the declaration says belongs there.\npub fn decode_command_demo_core_fire(_value: &json::Value, _at: &str) -> Result<demo_types::core::Fire, json::DecodeError> {\n    Ok(demo_types::core::Fire {\n    })\n}\n\n/// Writes the outcome of `demo.core.Fire` as JSON: the branch taken, what it published, and the declared\n/// refusal it carries where it carries one.\npub fn encode_outcome_demo_core_fire(value: &demo_types::core::FireOutcome, out: &mut String) {\n    out.push('{');\n    match value {\n        demo_types::core::FireOutcome::Done { out } => {\n            json::member(out, \"outcome\");\n            json::push_text(out, \"done\");\n            json::member(out, \"published\");\n            out.push('[');\n            out.push('{');\n            json::member(out, \"event\");\n            json::push_text(out, \"demo.core.Out\");\n            json::member(out, \"payload\");\n            encode_event_demo_core_out(out, out);\n            out.push('}');\n            out.push(']');\n        }\n    }\n    out.push('}');\n}\n",

  "crates/demo-server/src/worker.docs.md": {
    "path": "crates/demo-server/src/worker.docs.md",
    "contents": "<!--\ngenerated from demo v1\nmodel digest 57d2eb2785ad2fb21ed2085027df2fe64d2c13ba96a7c2c117fa9ceb3a33bcb3\ncontract digest slice-sha256/2:b13ddd0f4c243fbcbbce4d83a7b63a61419f752c65caa827b15acc1b3a3c99df\ndo not edit: regenerate with `ess generate`\n-->\n\n# core\n\n`demo.core` is one of demo's bounded contexts. [Back to the index](../index.md).\n\n## Commands\n\n### `Fire`\n\n`demo.core.Fire`.\n\nIt takes no input.\n\nIt has one outcome.\n\n**`done`** — The default branch, taken when no other outcome's condition matched. No entity in this specification changes. It emits `demo.core.Out`. A test reaches it by constructing an input that satisfies no other outcome's condition.\n\n## Events\n\n### `Out`\n\n`demo.core.Out`.\n\nIt carries nothing: the fact that it happened is the whole payload.\n\nEmitted by `demo.core.Fire` on its `done` outcome.\n\nNothing in this system reacts to it.\n\n\n---\n\nGenerated from demo v1 · model digest `57d2eb2785ad2fb21ed2085027df2fe64d2c13ba96a7c2c117fa9ceb3a33bcb3` · contract digest `slice-sha256/2:b13ddd0f4c243fbcbbce4d83a7b63a61419f752c65caa827b15acc1b3a3c99df`. Do not edit this file; change the specification and regenerate it with `ess generate`.\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"
    }
  },
  "crates/demo-server/src/worker.openapi.json": {
    "path": "crates/demo-server/src/worker.openapi.json",
    "contents": "{\n  \"openapi\": \"3.1.0\",\n  \"info\": {\n    \"title\": \"worker\",\n    \"description\": \"The HTTP surface of `worker`, one of the components of `demo` v1.\\n\\nEvery path here is one semantic command, so the method is always POST and the path is the command's wire name under its domain's: a command is not a resource, and this document does not invent one. A status code is the outcome the specification declares — 202 for a branch that was taken, 422 for a refusal the input decides, 502 for a refusal decided outside the request — and the `outcome` property of every response body names the branch. Events emitted by a branch are published to consumers through the event transport; they are not returned here.\\n\\nThis component declares `reached_by: network`, so its callers are not deployed with it and this contract is the surface they reach it through. That declaration is also why the views its domains declare have paths here: a GET under `views` per projection, answering every row it holds. There is no page size, no cursor, no ordering and no filter parameter, because the specification states none — a view's filter is part of the projection, not of the request.\",\n    \"version\": \"v1\",\n    \"x-ess-provenance\": {\n      \"system\": \"demo\",\n      \"specification_version\": \"v1\",\n      \"source_digest\": \"57d2eb2785ad2fb21ed2085027df2fe64d2c13ba96a7c2c117fa9ceb3a33bcb3\",\n      \"contract_digest\": \"slice-sha256/2:b13ddd0f4c243fbcbbce4d83a7b63a61419f752c65caa827b15acc1b3a3c99df\"\n    },\n    \"x-ess-reached-by\": \"network\"\n  },\n  \"tags\": [\n    {\n      \"name\": \"core\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"paths\": {\n    \"/core/commands/Fire\": {\n      \"post\": {\n        \"operationId\": \"demo.core.Fire\",\n        \"summary\": \"Fire\",\n        \"tags\": [\n          \"core\"\n        ],\n        \"responses\": {\n          \"202\": {\n            \"description\": \"Outcome `done`: the branch the specification declares for this input. Events this branch emits are published to consumers, not returned here.\",\n            \"content\": {\n              \"application/json\": {\n                \"schema\": {\n                  \"$ref\": \"#/components/schemas/demo.core.Fire.done.Response\"\n                }\n              }\n            }\n          }\n        }\n      }\n    }\n  },\n  \"components\": {\n    \"schemas\": {\n      \"demo.core.Fire.done.Response\": {\n        \"additionalProperties\": false,\n        \"description\": \"Taken when no other outcome's condition matched. Emits `Out`, published to consumers rather than returned here.\",\n        \"properties\": {\n          \"outcome\": {\n            \"const\": \"done\",\n            \"description\": \"Which declared outcome the command took.\"\n          }\n        },\n        \"required\": [\n          \"outcome\"\n        ],\n        \"type\": \"object\"\n      }\n    }\n  }\n}\n",
    "slice": {
      "of": "whole-model"

  "crates/demo-server/src/worker.rs": {
    "path": "crates/demo-server/src/worker.rs",
    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 57d2eb2785ad2fb21ed2085027df2fe64d2c13ba96a7c2c117fa9ceb3a33bcb3\n// contract digest b13ddd0f4c243fbcbbce4d83a7b63a61419f752c65caa827b15acc1b3a3c99df\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize`\n\n//! The `worker` component of `demo` v1, on the wire.\n//!\n//! The specification says this component's callers are not deployed with it, so its surface\n//! exists on a wire. Which wire is derived rather than chosen: the one contract this model\n//! projects for a command surface is the `OpenAPI` document, and an `OpenAPI` document is an\n//! HTTP contract. The document is beside this file, served verbatim at `/openapi.json`.\n\nuse crate::{http, json, wire};\n\n/// The contract this surface answers, byte for byte as `generated/` commits it.\n///\n/// Embedded rather than rebuilt at run time: a server that regenerated its own contract could\n/// publish one the repository never reviewed.\npub const OPENAPI: &str = include_str!(\"worker.openapi.json\");\n\n/// The prose the same model produced, byte for byte as the documentation projection wrote it.\npub const DOCS: &str = include_str!(\"worker.docs.md\");\n\n/// Every route this surface answers, in path order.\n///\n/// The same set the `OpenAPI` document declares, plus the two documents about the surface\n/// itself, which no specification construct names and nothing can therefore derive. A path\n/// absent from this table is answered with `404`, including one the document declares and this\n/// table forgot — which is the failure a table computed twice would hide.\npub const ROUTES: &[(&str, &str)] = &[\n    (\"POST\", \"/core/commands/Fire\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/docs\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/openapi.json\"),\n];\n\n/// What this process says about itself as it starts, before it answers anything.\n///\n/// Three lines of JSON on standard output, in this order, every member of them derived from the\n/// specification — except `runtime`, which is appended by the emitted code below and holds what\n/// is true of *this process*: the language it was synthesised into, and the address it bound.\n/// Everything outside `runtime` is the same in every language this plan is emitted into, and\n/// `cargo xtask synth --check` starts both and compares them.\npub const STARTUP: &[&str] = &[\n    \"{\\\"log\\\":\\\"ess/1\\\",\\\"event\\\":\\\"system.starting\\\",\\\"system\\\":\\\"demo\\\",\\\"version\\\":\\\"v1\\\",\\\"model_digest\\\":\\\"57d2eb2785ad2fb21ed2085027df2fe64d2c13ba96a7c2c117fa9ceb3a33bcb3\\\",\\\"contract_digest\\\":\\\"b13ddd0f4c243fbcbbce4d83a7b63a61419f752c65caa827b15acc1b3a3c99df\\\",\\\"components\\\":[\\\"worker\\\"],\\\"capabilities\\\":{\\\"generated\\\":4,\\\"obligations\\\":1,\\\"refused\\\":0}\",\n    \"{\\\"log\\\":\\\"ess/1\\\",\\\"event\\\":\\\"surface.serving\\\",\\\"component\\\":\\\"worker\\\",\\\"reached_by\\\":\\\"network\\\",\\\"transport\\\":\\\"http/1.1\\\",\\\"routes\\\":3,\\\"paths\\\":[{\\\"method\\\":\\\"POST\\\",\\\"path\\\":\\\"/core/commands/Fire\\\",\\\"serves\\\":\\\"command\\\",\\\"name\\\":\\\"demo.core.Fire\\\"},{\\\"method\\\":\\\"GET\\\",\\\"path\\\":\\\"/docs\\\",\\\"serves\\\":\\\"documentation\\\",\\\"name\\\":\\\"docs\\\"},{\\\"method\\\":\\\"GET\\\",\\\"path\\\":\\\"/openapi.json\\\",\\\"serves\\\":\\\"contract\\\",\\\"name\\\":\\\"openapi\\\"}]\",\n    \"{\\\"log\\\":\\\"ess/1\\\",\\\"event\\\":\\\"system.ready\\\",\\\"system\\\":\\\"demo\\\",\\\"surfaces\\\":1\",\n];\n\n/// Writes the startup record, with this process's own facts closing each line.\nfn announce(address: &std::net::SocketAddr) {\n    for facts in STARTUP {\n        let mut line = String::from(*facts);\n        line.push_str(\",\\\"runtime\\\":{\\\"address\\\":\");\n        json::push_text(&mut line, &address.to_string());\n        line.push_str(\",\\\"language\\\":\\\"rust\\\",\\\"port\\\":\");\n        json::push_integer(&mut line, i64::from(address.port()));\n        line.push_str(\"}}\");\n        println!(\"{line}\");\n    }\n}\n\n/// Serves `worker` at `address`, and does not return while it can answer.\n///\n/// `address` may name port `0`, which binds an ephemeral port; the startup record says which one\n/// was taken, because a caller that cannot learn the port cannot make a request.\n///\n/// It chooses no realization. Every command reaches the port, and a port over unimplemented\n/// obligations answers the typed refusal this surface reports as `501` — the honest empty\n/// state rather than a server that pretends.\n///\n/// # Errors\n///\n/// Anything the listener refuses: the address is taken, the port is privileged, the socket\n/// died.\npub fn serve<WorkerBehaviors>(system: &mut demo_system::System<WorkerBehaviors>, address: &str) -> std::io::Result<()>\nwhere\n    WorkerBehaviors: demo_types::core::obligations::FireBehavior,\n{\n    let listener = std::net::TcpListener::bind(address)?;\n    announce(&listener.local_addr()?);\n    for connection in listener.incoming() {\n        let mut reader = std::io::BufReader::new(connection?);\n        let answer = match http::read(&mut reader) {\n            Ok(request) => dispatch(system, &request),\n            Err(refusal) => refusal,\n        };\n        let mut stream = reader.into_inner();\n        http::write(&mut stream, &answer)?;\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n\n/// Answers one request.\n///\n/// A path this table does not hold is a `404` naming where the whole table is published; a\n/// path it holds under a different method is a `405` naming the one it answers. Neither is a\n/// status the contract declares, and neither should be: both are facts about a transport rather\n/// than about any command.\nfn dispatch<WorkerBehaviors>(system: &mut demo_system::System<WorkerBehaviors>, request: &http::Request) -> http::Response\nwhere\n    WorkerBehaviors: demo_types::core::obligations::FireBehavior,\n{\n    match request.path.as_str() {\n        \"/core/commands/Fire\" => {\n            if request.method != \"POST\" {\n                return http::method_not_allowed(\"POST\");\n            }\n            serve_demo_core_fire(system, &request.body)\n        }\n        \"/docs\" => {\n            if request.method != \"GET\" {\n                return http::method_not_allowed(\"GET\");\n            }\n            http::Response::new(200, http::MARKDOWN, DOCS)\n        }\n        \"/openapi.json\" => {\n            if request.method != \"GET\" {\n                return http::method_not_allowed(\"GET\");\n            }\n            http::Response::new(200, http::JSON, OPENAPI)\n        }\n        other => http::Response::refusal(\n            404,\n            &format!(\"`{other}` is not a path this surface declares; `GET /openapi.json` publishes every one that is\"),\n        ),\n    }\n}\n\n/// `POST` `demo.core.Fire`: reads the declared input, runs the port, answers the declared outcome.\nfn serve_demo_core_fire<WorkerBehaviors>(system: &mut demo_system::System<WorkerBehaviors>, body: &[u8]) -> http::Response\nwhere\n    WorkerBehaviors: demo_types::core::obligations::FireBehavior,\n{\n    let text = match std::str::from_utf8(body) {\n        Ok(text) => text,\n        Err(error) => {\n            return http::Response::refusal(400, &format!(\"the body is not UTF-8: {error}\"));\n        }\n    };\n    let value = match json::parse(text) {\n        Ok(value) => value,\n        Err(error) => {\n            return http::Response::refusal(400, &format!(\"the body is not JSON: {error}\"));\n        }\n    };\n    let input = match wire::decode_command_demo_core_fire(&value, \"body\") {\n        Ok(input) => input,\n        Err(error) => {\n            // `400` and not `422`: this is a body the schema decides, which is the difference\n            // between fixing a value and fixing a serialiser.\n            return http::Response::refusal(400, &format!(\"{error}\"));\n        }\n    };\n    match system.worker.fire(input) {\n        Ok(outcome) => answer_demo_core_fire(&outcome),\n        Err(unmet) => http::Response::refusal(501, &format!(\"{unmet}\")),\n    }\n}\n\n/// One declared outcome of `demo.core.Fire`, as the contract publishes it: the branch that was taken,\n/// the declared error where there is one, and that error's own payload.\nfn answer_demo_core_fire(outcome: &demo_types::core::FireOutcome) -> http::Response {\n    let mut body = String::from(\"{\");\n    let status = match outcome {\n        demo_types::core::FireOutcome::Done { .. } => {\n            json::member(&mut body, \"outcome\");\n            json::push_text(&mut body, \"done\");\n            202\n        }\n    };\n    body.push('}');\n    http::Response::new(status, http::JSON, body)\n}\n",

  "crates/demo-system/src/lib.rs": {
    "path": "crates/demo-system/src/lib.rs",
    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 57d2eb2785ad2fb21ed2085027df2fe64d2c13ba96a7c2c117fa9ceb3a33bcb3\n// contract digest b13ddd0f4c243fbcbbce4d83a7b63a61419f752c65caa827b15acc1b3a3c99df\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize`\n\n//! The `demo` system, v1: its components assembled, its bindings wired, and its one transport.\n//!\n//! The transport is derived from the specification, not chosen: `at_least_once` is the only\n//! delivery guarantee the model declares, so published events land on an append-only log and a\n//! pump delivers each to every binding that reacts to it. The log is the system's observable\n//! record, and so is the record of what each binding invoked. What no specification determines\n//! — how an escalation event is filled, behaviour behind the ports — stays an obligation; see\n//! the `PLAN.md` beside this workspace.\n\n#![forbid(unsafe_code)]\n#![deny(missing_docs)]\n\n/// An event on the system's log: everything any component publishes, and everything a binding\n/// escalates into.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum SystemEvent {\n    /// `demo.core.Out`.\n    Out(demo_types::core::Out),\n}\n\nimpl From<worker::PublishedEvent> for SystemEvent {\n    fn from(event: worker::PublishedEvent) -> Self {\n        match event {\n            worker::PublishedEvent::Out(event) => Self::Out(event),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// The `demo` system: every component behind its port, and the transport between them.\n///\n/// The component fields are public because commands enter the system through a component's own\n/// port; the log and its delivery cursor are not, because publishing happens by pumping, not by\n/// writing history directly.\npub struct System<WorkerBehaviors> {\n    /// The `worker` component.\n    pub worker: worker::Worker<WorkerBehaviors>,\n    published: Vec<SystemEvent>,\n    cursor: usize,\n}\n\nimpl<WorkerBehaviors> System<WorkerBehaviors> {\n    /// Assembles the system from its components.\n    pub fn new(worker: worker::Worker<WorkerBehaviors>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            worker,\n            published: Vec::new(),\n            cursor: 0,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Everything published so far, in publication order — the system's observable record.\n    pub fn published(&self) -> &[SystemEvent] {\n        &self.published\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<WorkerBehaviors> System<WorkerBehaviors>\nwhere\n    WorkerBehaviors: demo_types::core::obligations::FireBehavior,\n{\n    /// Delivers until quiescent: collects every component's outbox onto the log, then delivers\n    /// each logged event to every binding that reacts to it — at least once each, which is the\n    /// guarantee the specification declares.\n    ///\n    /// `Err` carries the first unmet obligation that delivery could not route around; the log\n    /// keeps everything already published. A specification whose bindings feed each other\n    /// without end will not quiesce, and this pump will not pretend otherwise.\n    pub fn pump(&mut self) -> Result<(), demo_types::obligation::UnmetObligation> {\n        loop {\n            self.collect();\n            if self.cursor == self.published.len() {\n                return Ok(());\n            }\n            self.cursor += 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Moves every component's outbox onto the log, in component order.\n    fn collect(&mut self) {\n        for event in self.worker.drain_outbox() {\n            self.published.push(SystemEvent::from(event));\n        }\n    }\n}\n",

Everything this bounded context declares that the synthesis plan marks generated.\n\n/// Fire — the input of `demo.core.Fire`.\n///\n/// Everything it can result in is [`FireOutcome`].\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Fire {\n}\n\n/// Everything `demo.core.Fire` can result in — one variant per declared outcome.\n///\n/// An infrastructure failure is deliberately not in here: a refusal is a fact about the domain,\n/// a transport fault is a fact about the run, and conflating the two is what the declared\n/// outcomes exist to prevent.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum FireOutcome {\n    /// `done` — otherwise.\n    Done {\n        /// The `demo.core.Out` this outcome publishes.\n        out: Out,\n    },\n}\n\n/// Out — the event `demo.core.Out`.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Out;\n\n/// What this bounded context owes its implementor, as typed seams.\n///\n/// One trait per obligation in the synthesis plan, each carrying the plan's own contract.\n/// [`Unimplemented`](obligations::Unimplemented) satisfies every trait by refusing in the type system, so the workspace builds —\n/// and says exactly what it cannot yet do — before a line is hand-written.\npub mod obligations {\n    /// The behaviour `demo.core.Fire` — an implementation obligation.\n    ///\n    /// Why it is not generated: the contract is declared; the algorithm is not.\n    ///\n    /// Contract: given `demo.core.Fire` input, decide and enact exactly one outcome — `done` otherwise, emits `demo.core.Out`.\n    pub trait FireBehavior {\n        /// Decides and enacts exactly one declared outcome of `demo.core.Fire`.\n        ///\n        /// `Err` is the typed refusal of an obligation nothing has satisfied; a satisfying\n        /// implementation never returns it.\n        fn fire(&mut self, input: super::Fire) -> Result<super::FireOutcome, crate::obligation::UnmetObligation>;\n    }\n\n    /// Every obligation of this bounded context, refused in the type system.\n    ///\n    /// Each method returns the typed refusal naming what is owed — never a panic, never a guessed\n    /// value — so a workspace built on this stub compiles and reports its own gaps.\n    pub struct Unimplemented;\n\n    impl FireBehavior for Unimplemented {\n        fn fire(&mut self, _input: super::Fire) -> Result<super::FireOutcome, crate::obligation::UnmetObligation> {\n            Err(crate::obligation::UnmetObligation { capability: \"command behaviour\", source: \"demo.core.Fire\" })\n        }\n    }\n}\n",

The typed refusal of an unmet obligation, and the conversion seams owed between contexts.\n//!\n//! An obligation is a capability the synthesis plan owes the implementor — the contract is declared,\n//! the behaviour is not. Until an implementation satisfies one, its stub returns [`UnmetObligation`]:\n//! a value naming the plan entry, never a panic and never a guess, so a workspace built on stubs\n//! compiles and reports its own gaps.\n\n/// A capability the synthesis plan owes and nothing has satisfied yet.\n///\n/// The two fields spell the plan entry: look the pair up in `PLAN.md` for the contract being\n/// refused. A satisfying implementation never constructs one.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct UnmetObligation {\n    /// The capability kind, as the plan spells it.\n    pub capability: &'static str,\n    /// The construct that requires it, in the specification's own spelling.\n    pub source: &'static str,\n}\n\nimpl core::fmt::Display for UnmetObligation {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut core::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> core::fmt::Result {\n        write!(f, \"unmet obligation: {} `{}` — see PLAN.md\", self.capability, self.source)\n    }\n}\n",

How the specification's primitives are spelled in this workspace.\n//!\n//! Four map onto types that already mean exactly the same thing: `String` stays `String`,\n//! `Boolean` is `bool`, `Integer` is `i64`, `Bytes` is `Vec<u8>`. The four below have no `std`\n//! equivalent, and no dependency is taken for them — this workspace builds from exactly its\n//! committed bytes. Each is a transparent wrapper over its wire rendering, distinct from `String`\n//! and from each other for the same reason the specification's own newtypes are distinct from\n//! their representations: a value's meaning is not its shape.\n\n/// An exact decimal, carried as its wire rendering — a decimal string such as `10.50`.\n///\n/// Never a float: money does not round the way a float does. Equality and order are over the\n/// rendering, so `1.5` and `1.50` are different values here; arithmetic is deliberately absent,\n/// because what a decimal *does* is behaviour, and behaviour is not synthesised.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]\npub struct Decimal(pub String);\n\n/// An instant, carried as its wire rendering — RFC 3339, such as `2026-01-01T00:00:00Z`.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]\npub struct Timestamp(pub String);\n\n/// A length of time, carried as its wire rendering — an ISO 8601 duration such as `P30D`.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]\npub struct Duration(pub String);\n\n/// A UUID, carried as its canonical textual rendering.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]\npub struct Uuid(pub String);\n",

    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 57d2eb2785ad2fb21ed2085027df2fe64d2c13ba96a7c2c117fa9ceb3a33bcb3\n// contract digest b13ddd0f4c243fbcbbce4d83a7b63a61419f752c65caa827b15acc1b3a3c99df\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize`\n\n//! worker — the `worker` component of `demo` v1.\n//!\n//! The component's outer surface exactly as the specification declares it: accepted commands as\n//! handlers, declared views as queries, published events as a typed outbox. The behaviour behind\n//! every handler is an implementation obligation — see the `PLAN.md` beside this workspace — and\n//! until one is satisfied, its stub answers with a typed refusal naming what is owed.\n\n#![forbid(unsafe_code)]\n#![deny(missing_docs)]\n\n/// An event this component declares it publishes, on its way to the system's transport.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum PublishedEvent {\n    /// `demo.core.Out`.\n    Out(demo_types::core::Out),\n}\n\n/// worker — the port over the component's obligations.\n///\n/// `B` bundles every behaviour and query this component owes; constructing it over the domain's\n/// `obligations::Unimplemented` yields a component that compiles and refuses, in the type system,\n/// everything not yet implemented.\npub struct Worker<B> {\n    behaviors: B,\n    outbox: Vec<PublishedEvent>,\n}\n\nimpl<B> Worker<B> {\n    /// A new port over the given obligation implementations.\n    pub fn new(behaviors: B) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            behaviors,\n            outbox: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Hands over everything published since the last drain, in publication order.\n    ///\n    /// The system's transport calls this; anything else reading it is taking events the transport\n    /// will then never deliver.\n    pub fn drain_outbox(&mut self) -> Vec<PublishedEvent> {\n        core::mem::take(&mut self.outbox)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<B> Worker<B>\nwhere\n    B: demo_types::core::obligations::FireBehavior,\n{\n    /// Accepts `demo.core.Fire`: runs the behaviour obligation, then publishes the declared events\n    /// the outcome carries.\n    ///\n    /// `Err` is the typed refusal of an unmet obligation — never a domain outcome, which always\n    /// arrives as a variant of the outcome type, refusals included.\n    pub fn fire(&mut self, input: demo_types::core::Fire) -> Result<demo_types::core::FireOutcome, demo_types::obligation::UnmetObligation> {\n        let outcome = self.behaviors.fire(input)?;\n        match &outcome {\n            demo_types::core::FireOutcome::Done { out, .. } => {\n                self.outbox.push(PublishedEvent::Out(out.clone()));\n            }\n        }\n        Ok(outcome)\n    }\n}\n",

The transport is derived from the specification, not chosen: `at_least_once` is the only\n//! delivery guarantee the model declares, so published events land on an append-only log and a\n//! pump delivers each to every binding that reacts to it. What no specification determines\n//! — how an escalation event is filled, behaviour behind the ports — stays an obligation; see\n//! the `PLAN.md` beside this workspace.\n\n#![forbid(unsafe_code)]\n#![deny(missing_docs)]\n\n/// An event on the system's log: everything any component publishes, and everything a binding\n/// escalates into.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum SystemEvent {\n    /// `demo.core.Fired`.\n    Fired(demo_types::core::Fired),\n}\n\nimpl From<json::PublishedEvent> for SystemEvent {\n    fn from(event: json::PublishedEvent) -> Self {\n        match event {\n            json::PublishedEvent::Fired(event) => Self::Fired(event),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// The `demo` system: every component behind its port, and the transport between them.\n///\n/// The component fields are public because commands enter the system through a component's own\n/// port; the log and its delivery cursor are not, because publishing happens by pumping, not by\n/// writing history directly.\npub struct System<JsonBehaviors> {\n    /// The `json` component.\n    pub json: json::Json<JsonBehaviors>,\n    published: Vec<SystemEvent>,\n    cursor: usize,\n}\n\nimpl<JsonBehaviors> System<JsonBehaviors> {\n    /// Assembles the system from its components.\n    pub fn new(json: json::Json<JsonBehaviors>) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            json,\n            published: Vec::new(),\n            cursor: 0,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Everything published so far, in publication order — the system's observable record.\n    pub fn published(&self) -> &[SystemEvent] {\n        &self.published\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<JsonBehaviors> System<JsonBehaviors>\nwhere\n    JsonBehaviors: demo_types::core::obligations::FireBehavior,\n{\n    /// Delivers until quiescent: collects every component's outbox onto the log, then delivers\n    /// each logged event to every binding that reacts to it — at least once each, which is the\n    /// guarantee the specification declares.\n    ///\n    /// `Err` carries the first unmet obligation that delivery could not route around; the log\n    /// keeps everything already published. A specification whose bindings feed each other\n    /// without end will not quiesce, and this pump will not pretend otherwise.\n    pub fn pump(&mut self) -> Result<(), demo_types::obligation::UnmetObligation> {\n        loop {\n            self.collect();\n            if self.cursor == self.published.len() {\n                return Ok(());\n            }\n            self.cursor += 1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Moves every component's outbox onto the log, in component order.\n    fn collect(&mut self) {\n        for event in self.json.drain_outbox() {\n            self.published.push(SystemEvent::from(event));\n        }\n    }\n}\n",

The component's outer surface exactly as the specification declares it: accepted commands as\n//! handlers, declared views as queries, published events as a typed outbox. The behaviour behind\n//! every handler is an implementation obligation — see the `PLAN.md` beside this workspace — and\n//! until one is satisfied, its stub answers with a typed refusal naming what is owed.\n\n#![forbid(unsafe_code)]\n#![deny(missing_docs)]\n\n/// An event this component declares it publishes, on its way to the system's transport.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum PublishedEvent {\n    /// `demo.core.Fired`.\n    Fired(demo_types::core::Fired),\n}\n\n/// json — the port over the component's obligations.\n///\n/// `B` bundles every behaviour and query this component owes; constructing it over the domain's\n/// `obligations::Unimplemented` yields a component that compiles and refuses, in the type system,\n/// everything not yet implemented.\npub struct Json<B> {\n    behaviors: B,\n    outbox: Vec<PublishedEvent>,\n}\n\nimpl<B> Json<B> {\n    /// A new port over the given obligation implementations.\n    pub fn new(behaviors: B) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            behaviors,\n            outbox: Vec::new(),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Hands over everything published since the last drain, in publication order.\n    ///\n    /// The system's transport calls this; anything else reading it is taking events the transport\n    /// will then never deliver.\n    pub fn drain_outbox(&mut self) -> Vec<PublishedEvent> {\n        core::mem::take(&mut self.outbox)\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<B> Json<B>\nwhere\n    B: demo_types::core::obligations::FireBehavior,\n{\n    /// Accepts `demo.core.Fire`: runs the behaviour obligation, then publishes the declared events\n    /// the outcome carries.\n    ///\n    /// `Err` is the typed refusal of an unmet obligation — never a domain outcome, which always\n    /// arrives as a variant of the outcome type, refusals included.\n    pub fn fire(&mut self, input: demo_types::core::Fire) -> Result<demo_types::core::FireOutcome, demo_types::obligation::UnmetObligation> {\n        let outcome = self.behaviors.fire(input)?;\n        match &outcome {\n            demo_types::core::FireOutcome::Done { fired, .. } => {\n                self.outbox.push(PublishedEvent::Fired(fired.clone()));\n            }\n        }\n        Ok(outcome)\n    }\n}\n",

    "contents": "// generated from demo v1\n// model digest 62d697a2dc63d3371dbfe8359ba1a954c8e4746c7aa7ccb91ce949e21fd474aa\n// contract digest 6fde7d411a8116f5742b1d94aeb9cc27ed1a2f65b6ac685a00b662ab3af90cef\n// do not edit: regenerate with `ess synthesize --target web`\n\n// The boundary, written once. The page imports it, and so does the smoke test that loads the\n// module outside a browser — so what the test exercises is the page's own glue and not a second\n// implementation that happens to agree with it.\n//\n// Three exports and nothing else. `memory.buffer` is read afresh on every access because\n// allocating inside the module may grow the memory, which detaches every view taken before it.\n\n/**\n * Instantiates a module and answers a driver over it.\n *\n * `source` is anything `WebAssembly.instantiate` accepts: the bytes of a `.wasm`, or a compiled\n * `Module`. If the module exports the optional realization hook, it is called once, before any\n * request — that is how a host that linked implementations of the plan's obligations reaches a\n * page neither of them was written against.\n */\nexport async function open(source) {\n  const { instance } = await WebAssembly.instantiate(source, {});\n  const exports = instance.exports;\n  for (const name of EXPORTS) {\n    if (typeof exports[name] !== \"function\") {\n      throw new Error(`the module does not export ${name}; the page and the module disagree`);\n    }\n  }\n  const realized = typeof exports[REALIZE] === \"function\";\n  if (realized) exports[REALIZE]();\n\n  const encoder = new TextEncoder();\n  const decoder = new TextDecoder();\n\n  function request(body) {\n    const bytes = encoder.encode(JSON.stringify(body));\n    const address = exports.ess_input_reserve(bytes.length);\n    new Uint8Array(exports.memory.buffer, address, bytes.length).set(bytes);\n    const at = exports.ess_dispatch();\n    const length = exports.ess_output_len();\n    const text = decoder.decode(new Uint8Array(exports.memory.buffer, at, length));\n    return JSON.parse(text);\n  }\n\n  return { request, realized, exports };\n}\n\nexport const EXPORTS = [\"ess_input_reserve\", \"ess_dispatch\", \"ess_output_len\"];\n\nexport const REALIZE = \"ess_realize\";\n",

The page asks the running\n//! system for it — a page opened from `file://` can read its own WebAssembly module and cannot\n//! always read its neighbours.\n\n/// The model, as canonical JSON.\npub const CATALOG: &str = include_str!(\"../../../catalog.json\");\n",
